Prove the constructor

The constructor and its full structural proof
HILBERT 24 — PROVE THE CONSTRUCTOR
Full structural proof and original-source return
Collected 25 November 2026

The central theorem is a theorem about the generator. Its universal minimum
statement follows from that theorem and the local calibration invariant.
There is no separate procedure comparing the generated proof with competing
proofs. The full recovered sources accompany this explanation.

1. THE ORIGINAL RULE CONSTRUCTORS

Let J be the type of fully marked original judgements. Each rule r has a
conclusion h(r):J, a finite ordered arity a(r), and premises p(r,i):J for
i:Fin(a(r)). Contexts, original variable marks, evidence rails and any
resource conditions remain in J and in the typed rule.

The original finite derivation family is generated by

  node_r : (product_(i<a(r)) Derivation(p(r,i))) -> Derivation(h(r)).

Equivalently, for the polynomial family

  F(X)(j) = sum_(r:h(r)=j) product_(i<a(r)) X(p(r,i)),

Derivation is its inductive family mu F. This specifies constructors; it
does not ask for a list of all derivations. Rule and judgement types may be
infinite. Every individual rule has finite ordered arity.

For the natural-valued constructor in Hilbert24_Calibrated_Policy.lean, a
calibration supplies rule charge c(r), potential V(j), and slack s(r):Nat
with the exact local equation

  c(r) + sum_i V(p(r,i)) = V(h(r)) + s(r).                 (1)

Define work and total slack by the same original rule constructors:

  W(node_r(children)) = c(r) + sum_i W(children(i)),
  S(node_r(children)) = s(r) + sum_i S(children(i)).        (2)

Premise occurrences are counted with their original multiplicity. Sharing
the representation of a child does not silently change this objective.

2. THE STRUCTURAL INVARIANT

For every original derivation p:Derivation(j),

  W(p) = V(j) + S(p).                                    (3)

PROOF. Use structural induction on the derivation constructor. At a node
node_r(children), the induction hypothesis for each original child gives

  W(node_r(children))
    = c(r) + sum_i [V(p(r,i)) + S(children(i))]
    = [c(r) + sum_i V(p(r,i))] + sum_i S(children(i))
    = V(h(r)) + [s(r) + sum_i S(children(i))]
    = V(h(r)) + S(node_r(children)).

The third equality is precisely (1). Empty sums give the same calculation
for a nullary rule. These are all constructors, so the induction is complete.
Because S(p) is natural-valued, (3) immediately gives V(j) <= W(p).

The original Agda/CASL version proves the corresponding excess identity in
an explicitly supplied abelian charge group with a pointed additive
positive cone. Its full proof is retained separately; a total order is not
required for that comparison theorem.

3. DEFINE AND PROVE THE GENERATOR

A TightPolicy is concrete local construction data. For each j it supplies:

  chosen(j): an actual rule with conclusion j;
  tight(j):  s(chosen(j)) = 0;
  rank(j):   a natural number;
  decrease:  rank(p(chosen(j),i)) < rank(j) for every premise i.

Define the generator by well-founded recursion:

  build(j) = node_(chosen(j))
               (i |-> build(p(chosen(j),i))).              (4)

THEOREM. For every j in the policy domain, (4) constructs a finite original
derivation of j and satisfies

  S(build(j)) = 0,
  W(build(j)) = V(j).                                     (5)

PROOF. Induct on rank(j). Every recursive call in (4) has strictly smaller
rank, so its result exists, is a finite derivation of its typed premise,
and has zero total slack by the induction hypothesis. The actual original
rule constructor combines those results into a derivation of j. Its slack is

  S(build(j)) = s(chosen(j))
                 + sum_i S(build(p(chosen(j),i)))
             = 0 + sum_i 0 = 0.

Equation (3), applied to that constructed term, now yields W(build(j))=V(j).
The nullary case has no recursive calls and the same zero-slack equation.
This proves termination, typing, and the exact grade of the generator.

COROLLARY. For an arbitrary q:Derivation(j),

  W(build(j)) = V(j) <= V(j)+S(q) = W(q).                  (6)

This is the universal minimum proof. The variable q is an arbitrary input
to a proof function. No enumeration, bounded search, or comparison pass is
part of (4), (5), or (6).

The recovered implementation packages these fields directly:

  derivation := build policy j
  exact_cost := build_attains policy j
  optimal    := build_is_global_minimum policy j

The source names are build, build_has_zero_slack, build_attains,
build_is_global_minimum, and constructMinimum. No attaining proof is passed
into constructMinimum; the attaining derivation is its output.

4. RETURN THE ORIGINAL MATHEMATICAL OBJECT

Let Meaning(j) be the original dependent target, and let each original rule
have its actual semantic constructor

  K_r : (product_i Meaning(p(r,i))) -> Meaning(h(r)).

Define interpret recursively using K_r at the same node. Induction on the
constructed derivation proves that every child has the required original
meaning and that K_r returns Meaning(h(r)). Therefore

  returnOriginal(j) = interpret(build(j)) : Meaning(j).    (7)

This is a typed proof-bearing return. Its source is the generated derivation
and the original rule constructors. A numerical minimum is not substituted
for the returned object. The source implements this as interpret and
returnOriginal.

5. THE CONCRETE SOURCE POLICY IS ALSO CONSTRUCTED

Hilbert24_Construct_Minimum.lean specializes the preceding generator to an
original Boolean verifier V:Bits(n)->Bool. Its original candidate family is

  Candidate(V) = fallback
                 | satisfying(a, proof that V(a)=true).

The declared candidate-code grade is n+2 for fallback and n+1 for a verified
original model. These are the objective values used in this specialization.

Define the exact residual source families

  E1(V)(a) = V(0,a) OR V(1,a),
  E2(V)(a) = V(0,0,a) OR V(0,1,a)
               OR V(1,0,a) OR V(1,1,a).

The source policy is defined by width:

  width 0:     evaluate the closed V(()) and construct its leaf;
  width 1:     choose splitOne, with its one premise (0,E1(V));
  width n+2:   choose splitTwo, with its one premise (n,E2(V)).

Each positive-width rule has one recursive premise containing the complete
residual verifier. The positive-width rule choice does not read sourceTag,
receive a SAT bit, or receive an already computed optimum. The rank is the
remaining original width, so its strict decrease is immediate.

For the invariant, define tau_0(V)=V(()) and
tau_(n+1)(V)=tau_n(E1(V)). Reassociation of Boolean OR proves

  tau_(n+2)(V) = tau_n(E2(V)).

The calibration potential is

  Phi(n,V) = n + (if tau_n(V) then 1 else 2).

The model-leaf calibration uses the following structural lemma. If V(a)=true,
then tau_n(V)=true. Induct on n. At zero, a=(). At a successor write a=(b,a');
the disjunction E1(V)(a') contains the true cofactor V(b,a'), so it is true.
Apply the induction hypothesis to the residual verifier E1(V). This is the
forward direction of sourceTag_true in the original formal proof.

The source charge is 1 for splitOne and 2 for splitTwo. Their sourceTag
identities prove local calibration with zero slack. The preceding lemma
shows that a verified model rule has potential n+1, hence its charge n+1
has zero slack. The fallback rule has charge n+2 and slack
1 when tau is true, otherwise 0. At width zero the policy chooses the model
leaf exactly when V(()) is true; otherwise it chooses the zero-slack fallback
leaf. Thus every selected rule is proved tight.

The selected original lifts are constructive. For E1, choose bit 0 if its
cofactor is true, otherwise bit 1. For E2, select the first true cofactor in
the fixed order 00,01,10,11, selecting 11 when all four are false. The local
selector identities prove that the selected original cofactor equals E1(V)
or E2(V). Hence a residual verified model lifts to a verified original model.
Fallback lifts to fallback. The grades satisfy

  grade(liftOne(V,p)) = 1 + grade(p),
  grade(liftTwo(V,p)) = 2 + grade(p).

Consequently the concrete output G obeys the actual recursive equations

  G(1,V)   = liftOne(V,G(0,E1(V))),
  G(n+2,V) = liftTwo(V,G(n,E2(V))).                        (8)

The source proves these as constructed_one and constructed_two. The generic
rank induction now applies to the concrete sourcePolicy, so the generator
constructs its derivation with exact cost Phi(n,V). Structural interpretation
and decode_grade prove

  grade(G(n,V)) = Phi(n,V).                               (9)

For any original candidate q, embed(q) is an original source derivation,
decode(embed(q))=q, and work(embed(q))=grade(q). Applying (6) to this arbitrary
embedded candidate proves the original-fibre minimum of G(n,V). This is the
proof implemented in constructed_original_minimum.

6. THE ORIGINAL CERTIFICATE IS CONSTRUCTED TOO

If G returns satisfying(a,h), return the original model a with h.
If G returns fallback, take an arbitrary original a. Case analysis on V(a)
has two cases. If V(a)=false, that is the desired proof. If V(a)=true, the
original candidate satisfying(a,h) has grade n+1, while the generated minimum
has grade n+2. Equation (6) would give n+2 <= n+1, a contradiction. Thus
V(a)=false for that arbitrary a, and abstraction gives forall a,V(a)=false.

This is certificateFromMinimum. It constructs the universally quantified
original rejection function without iterating through assignments. The
original-CNF adapter uses the same original clause evaluator and proves
original_CNF_sat_return and original_CNF_unsat_return.

The grade in (9) is the declared candidate-code objective. Actual producer
bit-step work retains its separate trace definition, acquisition charges,
arithmetic, verification and output. The collected amortization proof gives

  ActualWork(trace) + finalCredit
    = AmortizedWork(trace) + initialCredit,

by induction on the actual trace. This keeps the original cost meanings
unchanged while proving the generator above.

7. THE WHOLE MINIMUM FAMILY HAS ITS OWN CONSTRUCTORS

Once (5) supplies an attainer, (3) and (6) prove

  p is minimum  iff S(p)=0  iff every occurrence of p is tight.

Thus the complete minimum family has the inductive presentation

  Min(j) = mu X.
    sum_(r:h(r)=j and s(r)=0) product_i X(p(r,i)).           (10)

This is a constructor characterization of all ties. It is not a list of
them. Zero-cost cyclic rules may remain in this inductive presentation;
only finite grounded derivations are inhabitants. The selected ranked
policy constructs one inhabitant without deleting other tied constructors.

8. NATIVE TORSER AND INSTITUTIONAL RETURN

Keep each source judgement and its complete graded derivation/proof packet
in its native image. Let E_j,D_j encode and decode those packets, inverse on
that image. They act on the derivation family, before semantic interpretation
can identify different derivations. Let native rule operations K^N_r satisfy
the actual rule square

  D_(h(r))(K^N_r(n_i)) = node_r(D_(p(r,i))(n_i)).           (11)

Define nativeBuild by the same chosen rule and rank recursion, using K^N_r.
Rank induction, (11), and the induction hypothesis for each child prove

  D_j(nativeBuild(j)) = build(j).                         (12)

Applying the original interpreter to (12) then gives

  interpret(D_j(nativeBuild(j))) = returnOriginal(j).

If native grade is the original derivation grade pulled through D_j, the same induction
preserves (5) and (6). This proves the native generator and its return with
the rule operations themselves. Each institution's typed square can be used
independently; the source target is unchanged.

The collection also retains the explicit six-frame Gram-dagger sources,
X^dagger=J^(-1) X^T J, the integral odd-denominator involution resolvent,
the full cubical contraction and its unique anchored primitive, the original
Cech returns, and the proof-valued partition/fold equations. Their exact
carriers and hypotheses remain attached to their original full proofs.

CONSTRUCTIVE HILBERT 24: SOURCE THEOREMS AND REPLAY COMPOSITION

1. THE PRECISE ORIGINAL INTERFACE

Hilbert24_Portable.het retains seven original programme specifications unchanged: OrderedChargeGroup, CalibrationSteps, HCProofData, HCWholeProofReturns, HCMinimumReturn, HCNativeProofData, and HCNativeMinimumReturns. Additional nodes expose local constructor cases and independent charge identities. The original %implied declarations remain theorem goals.

The source is Final_Proofs_Seven_Twenty_GRH_YM_Hilbert24_PNP.het from the existing upload at /workspace/scratch/c6ef42ab8b45/upload/. Its explicit source labels are S001:3378 (charge group), S001:3398 (calibration), S001:4064--4148 (proof syntax and minimum return). The corresponding physical source blocks start at lines 3403, 3423, 4086, 4120, 4137, 4150, and 4170.

Let A be the declared abelian charge group with an additively closed pointed positive cone. Write a<=b when b-a is nonnegative. Pointedness says that a,b>=0 and a+b=0 imply a=b=0. This suffices; no total order or real-number comparison is assumed.

A rule consists of a conclusion J, a charge w, and a finite ordered list of premise judgements K_1,...,K_m. The family of admitted judgements and rules may be infinite. A proof is the original finite hcStep constructor with an ordered forest of child proofs. Every repeated premise occurrence stays present.

A calibration is an explicit potential phi on judgements such that, for every admitted rule,
  epsilon(r)=w+sum_i phi(K_i)-phi(J)>=0.
This is the only rule-wise lower-bound premise. Rule charges themselves need not be nonnegative for the theorem in this section; nonnegative charges are a separate sufficient condition in the finite cyclic optimizer.

2. CONSTRUCTIVE WHOLE-PROOF TELESCOPE

Define W(empty forest)=0; W(p::ps)=W(p)+W(ps); W(step(J,w,ps))=w+W(ps).
Define E(empty forest)=0; E(p::ps)=E(p)+E(ps); E(step(J,w,ps))=epsilon(rule)+E(ps).

Simultaneous structural induction on the free proof/forest syntax proves
  E(ps)=W(ps)-sum_(p in ps) phi(conclusion(p)),
  E(p)=W(p)-phi(conclusion(p)).

For the empty forest both sides are zero. For a cons, add the two induction equations and use
  (a-b)+(c-d)=(a+c)-(b+d).
For a tree, let A be the sum of its premise potentials. Substitution gives
  E(step)=(w+A-phi(J))+(W(ps)-A)
         =w+W(ps)-phi(J)
         =W(step)-phi(J).
Every internal premise potential is canceled by its own child's return; no occurrence is deleted.

A second simultaneous induction proves nonnegativity for valid proofs and forests. At a tree, validity supplies the admitted-rule condition; calibration gives epsilon>=0, the forest induction gives E(ps)>=0, and cone closure gives their sum>=0.

A third simultaneous induction proves, for valid syntax,
  E(p)=0 iff every rule occurrence is tight (epsilon=0),
and the analogous forest assertion. At each sum, pointedness of the positive cone says that a zero sum of nonnegative summands has both summands zero. The empty forest is tight by definition. No decision of an infinite family occurs.

The first two inductions and the definition of <= yield
  phi(J)<=W(p)
for EVERY admitted original finite proof p of J. This is the original unbounded HCProof quantifier; there is no fixed proof depth, coefficient aperture, or competitor count.

3. ATTAINED GLOBAL MINIMUM

Given an actual valid proof p* of J whose excess is zero, the telescope gives W(p*)=phi(J). Apply the lower bound to an arbitrary valid original competitor q of the same J:
  W(p*)=phi(J)<=W(q).
Thus p* satisfies exactly hcMinimum, whose definition quantifies over every original valid HCProof with that conclusion. Replacing zero excess by local tightness gives the second original minimum theorem.

This theorem constructs a minimum certificate from a calibration and a valid tight proof. It does not assume the global minimum property. When a tight proof p* exists, it also characterizes the entire minimum fibre: every tight valid proof has cost phi(J), hence is minimum; every minimum q has W(q)<=W(p*)=phi(J) and phi(J)<=W(q), so pointedness forces equality and hence zero excess. All ties remain.

4. EXACT NATIVE RETURN

Write E and D for the declared proof encoding/decoding, with DE=id and ED=id on the typed native image. Native validity, original judgement, and original complete charge are defined by D.

If p is minimum, let x be any native valid competitor of the same native conclusion as E(p). D(x) is a valid original competitor of the same original J, so W(p)<=W(D(x)). The grade definitions and DE=id rewrite this to nativeW(E(p))<=nativeW(x).

Conversely, if E(p) is native minimum, encode any original competitor q. Validity, judgement, and grade return under DE=id, so native minimality gives W(p)<=W(q). This proves nativeMinimum(E(p)) iff minimum(p). Both actual proof and every competitor return, including ties.

This proof uses the actual typed inverse image. For semantic institutional transport one still retains the relevant signature, sentence and model-reduct satisfaction square; a satisfaction comorphism alone is not a definition of an arbitrary proof constructor. The extracted source uses its explicit proof maps.

5. INDEPENDENT FIRST-ORDER REPLAY AND CONSTRUCTIVE SCOPE

The replay exports the exact CASL goals with Hets 0.108.0 and runs the existing E prover. The final batch has 18 proved first-order obligations: 15 have no theorem-lemma assumptions; 3 use already independently proved charge lemmas. Every successful export has its TPTP input, E arguments, E proof object, and Hets transcript saved. Replay_Results.json and Replay_Results.tsv identify each one.

The three checked dependencies are:
  h24_local_charge_cancellation <- h24_charge_internal_boundary_cancels;
  h24_excess_cons_step <- h24_charge_pair_telescopes;
  h24_excess_tree_step <- h24_local_charge_cancellation.
Each dependency is proved earlier in the replay, and its complete proof object is retained. The latter two local steps have exactly the same statements as their original HCInductionSteps counterparts.

The replay guards against any unproved %implied declaration appearing among the assumptions. This matters because this Hets version can import earlier-node theorem declarations as assumptions even with 'include-theorems false'. Definition-only replay nodes avoid those imported goals; only the three explicitly checked dependencies are admitted.

The six universal HCWholeProofReturns conclusions follow by the displayed simultaneous structural inductions using the checked base and constructor cases. They are composed induction results, not first-order E results pretending to include an induction rule. The two original minimum results then follow by the attainment/comparison proof, and the three native-return results follow by the explicit inverse argument and composition. E's classical proof format is an additional local verification; the construction above uses group equations, finite structural induction, supplied rule inequalities, and actual proof witnesses, with no choice or excluded-middle step.

Hets/E integration status: the exact E invocations print '# SZS status Theorem' and actual proof objects. This Hets/E version pair nevertheless leaves the interactive Hets goal flag 'still open'. The package reports the E result separately and does not claim that the Hets development graph stored a proved flag. The original theorem goals remain visibly %implied in the source for portable replay by another supported backend.

6. EXECUTABLE REPLAY

Set H24_HETS_RUNTIME to a Hets runtime root containing usr/lib/hets/hets-server and usr/bin/eprover if the recovered default path is unavailable. Then run:
  python3 formal/hets/replay_hets.py

The script checks exact exported formulas, preserves evidence, and records checked dependencies. It proves the pure group helpers before replaying dependent steps. Initial resource-limited attempts are historical intermediate runs; the final result manifest covers the resolved cases.
2. Constructive Hilbert 24: total generator, complete minimum fibre, finite cyclic calibration, and native return

Complete original source: Hilbert24_Constructive_Core.zip :: Hilbert24_Constructive_Core/Hilbert24_Constructive_Proof.txt

Arbitrary judgement/rule types, possibly infinite, with finite ordered arity and natural-number rule charge. Calibration identity c(r)+sum_i V(p(r,i))=V(h(r))+sigma(r), with natural slack. Actual selected tight rule at each policy judgement, natural rank decreasing along every selected premise. Original proof interpretation requires actual typed semantic constructors for the source rules. Automatic finite solver additionally requires a complete finite set of fully marked judgements and all original rules, with exact nonnegative rational charges. Cycle erasure substitutes only identical full judgements including contexts/resource state; charge is occurrence-additive. Native transport requires exact proof- and grade-preserving maps on the typed native image.

THEOREM 1: discrete Stokes identity; THEOREM 2: well-founded tight policy constructs global minimum; LEMMA 3: d_h gives minimum up to height h; LEMMA 4: cycle erasure gives a no-larger-cost proof of height at most m; THEOREM 3: d_m is the global finite-presentation minimum; complete_minimum_fibre; constructMinimum; returnOriginal; minimum transport equation (9)

CONSTRUCTIVE HILBERT 24
Original typed proof, exact grade, complete minimum fibre, normalized native return
24 November 2026 — America/Halifax

MAIN RESULT

The constructive core now has a total proof-producing operation. Given actual
typed inference rules, a locally calibrated potential, and a selected tight
rule at each judgement with a decreasing natural rank, it returns

  (p_J, W(p_J)=V(J), forall q:P(J), W(p_J)<=W(q)).

The operation is defined by well-founded recursion on original rule premises.
It admits arbitrary, including infinite, types of judgements and rules. Each
rule has finite ordered arity. No proof-depth, term-count, alternative-count,
node-budget or coefficient-aperture limit occurs in the theorem.

The full family of minima is exactly the inductively generated family of
finite proofs using only tight rules. The operation chooses one attainer while
retaining this whole family.

For a complete finite presentation with nonnegative exact rational charges,
the recovered cyclic tropical fold explicitly constructs the potential, tight
rules and decreasing policy. It operates on a finite recursive presentation of
an unbounded proof family. Its termination bound is proved by cycle erasure.

The main calibrated-policy theorem, all-minima characterization, and typed
original interpretation compile in Lean 4.19.0. The main and supporting files
print 28 declarations with empty axiom dependency lists. The Hets companion
records the portable algebraic interface and explicit induction obligations;
18 Hets-exported first-order obligations have E theorem proof objects.
The finite solver has a mathematical correctness proof and independently
checked result certificates; its Python implementation is not itself claimed
to have been verified by Lean.

1. FIX THE ORIGINAL QUESTION AND THE SIMPLICITY CRITERION

Let J be a type of fully marked judgements. A judgement includes every datum
needed for the original rule application: source signature, context, formula,
mode, polarity, resource state and dependent parameters where relevant.
Identifying two small matrix pictures does not identify different judgements.

Let R be a type of original rules. Rule r has

  h(r) : J,                     its conclusion;
  ar(r) : Nat,                  its finite ordered arity;
  p(r,i) : J, i<ar(r),          its premise judgements;
  c(r) : Nat,                   its declared nonnegative charge.

The original finite derivation family is the indexed inductive type

  P(j) = mu X. sum_(r:h(r)=j) product_(i<ar(r)) X(p(r,i)).

Opening a proof at its root returns the actual rule and ordered children.
Closing that rule reconstructs the proof. For dependent introduction rules,
retain the required witness or substitution in the rule index and its types;
do not replace a dependent fibre by an unrelated Cartesian product.

The declared grade is

  W(node(r,children)) = c(r) + sum_i W(children(i)).

This definition charges original occurrences. Two equal premise judgements
still occupy two slots. A shared evaluation table can reuse their computation
without changing this proof-tree objective.

The theorem is independent of which object logic supplies these constructors.
In particular, positive and opposing Belnap evidence can have separate marked
judgement families. An empty positive proof family does not manufacture an
opposing witness. The constructive metatheory uses inductive types, equality,
finite sums and natural recursion; the mathematical structure and rule
interpretations remain explicit inputs.

2. THE DISCRETE STOKES IDENTITY

A calibration supplies a potential V:J->Nat and local nonnegative slack
sigma:R->Nat satisfying, for every original rule,

  c(r) + sum_i V(p(r,i)) = V(h(r)) + sigma(r).             (1)

Equivalently, provide V(h(r))<=c(r)+sum_i V(p(r,i)); the slack is the natural
difference. The Lean function calibrationFromFeasible constructs this
difference and proves (1).

For any finite original derivation p, define

  S(node(r,children)) = sigma(r) + sum_i S(children(i)).

THEOREM 1. For every original derivation p:P(j),

  W(p) = V(j) + S(p).                                     (2)

PROOF. Induct on p. At a root r, apply the child identities:

  W(p)
    = c(r) + sum_i [V(p(r,i)) + S(children(i))]
    = [c(r) + sum_i V(p(r,i))] + sum_i S(children(i))
    = V(h(r)) + [sigma(r) + sum_i S(children(i))]
    = V(j) + S(p).

The nullary case uses the empty sum, equal to zero. Every premise occurrence
is counted in both sides of the cancellation. No ordering of an infinite
proof family, enumeration of competitors, or choice principle is used.

COROLLARY 1. V(j)<=W(p) for every p:P(j), because S(p)>=0.

COROLLARY 2. An actual proof p with S(p)=0 attains this lower bound and is
globally minimal among all original derivations of j.

This is the exact all-original-competitor statement recovered from
HCProofData, HCWholeProofReturns and HCMinimumReturn. The scope is the entire
declared original rule calculus. A calibration only on a smaller chart proves
the lower bound there; to use it for a larger source, provide either its
original-rule calibration or complete grade-preserving proof transport.

3. CONSTRUCT THE ATTAINER

A tight policy is explicit data:

  a(j) : an original rule with conclusion j;
  sigma(a(j))=0;
  rank:J->Nat;
  rank(p(a(j),i)) < rank(j), for every premise i.

Define

  build(j) =
    node(a(j), i |-> build(p(a(j),i))).                    (3)

The rank is a termination proof for the chosen recursion. It is derived from
the policy's original premise relation. It is not an externally imposed
search aperture. At a nullary rule there are no recursive calls.

THEOREM 2. Equation (3) defines a finite original derivation at every j in
the policy domain, and

  S(build(j))=0,
  W(build(j))=V(j),
  forall p:P(j), W(build(j))<=W(p).                        (4)

PROOF. Well-founded recursion on rank gives the typed derivation. Induction
on the same rank shows that each chosen root and all child occurrences have
zero slack. Equation (2) gives exact grade. Corollary 1 compares it with every
competitor.

The Lean function constructMinimum packages the actual derivation and both
certificates into OptimalReturn. It receives local construction data, not a
pre-existing minimum proof. A policy for an arbitrary source is not assumed
to follow just from writing its target formula. Section 5 gives an algorithm
that actually constructs the data for complete finite presentations.

Actual mathematical meaning is retained by a dependent family Meaning:J->Type
and, for each rule, its original typed constructor

  meaning_r : (product_i Meaning(p(r,i))) -> Meaning(h(r)).

The interpreter is structural recursion:

  interpret(node(r,children)) =
    meaning_r(i |-> interpret(children(i))).

Then returnOriginal(j)=interpret(build(j)):Meaning(j). This is the precise
proof-bearing return; the numeric grade alone is not used as a proof term.

4. THE WHOLE MINIMUM FIBRE AND THE PROOF-PATH INTEGRAL

Suppose the calibrated bound is attained, as in Theorem 2. For any p:P(j),

  p globally minimal
    iff W(p)=V(j)
    iff S(p)=0
    iff every rule occurrence in p has sigma=0.           (5)

For the first implication, compare p with build(j) and combine with the
universal lower bound. For the last implication, a finite sum of nonnegative
integers is zero exactly when each summand is zero. Structural induction
applies this at every node. The Lean development proves both directions and
the local allTight predicate.

Consequently the complete minimum family has the recursive presentation

  Min(j) =
    mu X. sum_(r:h(r)=j, sigma(r)=0)
             product_(i<ar(r)) X(p(r,i)).                 (6)

This retains all tied proof paths. Zero-cost recursive rules remain in (6).
A finite traversal followed by a genuine base constructor can be a minimum.
An ungrounded infinite self-loop is not an inhabitant of this inductive type.
Coinductive target proofs have their own specified soundness condition and
can enter the finite calculus through a sound, finitely justified rule.

For proof-valued coefficient families, put

  C_j(k) = {p:P(j) together with W(p)=k}.

Opening the root gives the explicit equivalence

  C_j(k) ~=
    sum_(r:h(r)=j)
    sum_(k_1,...,k_ar(r): c(r)+sum_i k_i=k)
      product_i C_(p(r,i))(k_i).                          (7)

These are tagged sums and ordered products of actual proof fibres.
Equation (7) is the constructive proof-path integral. Its tropical grade
forgets everything except the least occupied grade. Its ground fibre retains
the original proofs at that grade. In the present construction,

  Ground_j = C_j(V(j)) ~= Min(j).

Categorically this is the grade fibre of the indexed initial proof algebra.
It can be read internally in a higher topos with the indicated indexed
W-types and natural numbers object: sums and products are dependent, and
the fibre retains the comparison paths of its proof objects. Equivalences
of graded proof families restrict to equivalences of these full fibres.
The Lean file checks the displayed inductive type-theory construction.

With zero-cost loops, C_j(k) may contain infinitely many finite proofs.
It is therefore unsafe to assume its scalar cardinality is a finite
coefficient or that a real partition sum converges. For a finite rule
alphabet, one may retain a node-count marker:

  Z_j(t,u) = sum_(p:P(j)) t^(W(p)) u^(number_of_nodes(p)).

Each coefficient at a fixed cost and node count is then finite. The
proof-valued definition (7) needs no analytic convergence at all.

For a family P(j), each minimum attainer returned by (3) is a dependent
section. The entire fibre in (6) transports canonically. A selected point
also depends on its selection policy. Under a symmetry interchanging two
tied proofs, an invariant single point need not exist; transporting the
chosen original policy supplies coherent selections through the frames.

5. AUTOMATIC CONSTRUCTION ON COMPLETE FINITE PRESENTATIONS

The recovered finite_calibration routine accepts a finite explicit set of
fully marked judgements and, for each judgement, its complete finite list of
original rules. It allows cycles and exact nonnegative rational charges.
The proof family generated by this finite presentation can be infinite.

Let m be the number of judgements. Use infinity for an empty proof fibre:

  d_0(j)=infinity,
  d_(h+1)(j)=min_(r:h(r)=j) [c(r)+sum_i d_h(p(r,i))].       (8)

The empty sum is zero; an empty minimum is infinity. The implementation
represents infinity by None. Each round evaluates rule expressions and
retains their marks. It does not enumerate expanded proof trees.

LEMMA 3. d_h(j) is the minimum of all proofs of height at most h.

PROOF. Induct on h. A proof of height at most h+1 consists of a root rule
and children of height at most h. Each finite alternative takes the sum of
its child minima; closing its original rule gives attainment. Take the finite
minimum across rules. A missing child makes that alternative unavailable.

LEMMA 4. Every finite proof has a competitor of no larger cost and height at
most m.

PROOF. If a branch repeats the same fully marked judgement, replace the
ancestor subtree by the descendant subtree at that identical judgement.
The replacement is well-typed. Only nonnegative charged occurrences are
removed, so cost cannot increase. Node count strictly decreases. Repeating
this explicit transformation terminates by induction on node count.
In the resulting tree no branch repeats a judgement, so height is at most m.

This argument requires actual equality of the full judgement and context.
For resource-sensitive or value-dependent rules, retain those data in the
state or prove a substitutivity comparison. A coarse name is not sufficient.

THEOREM 3. L(j)=d_m(j) is the global minimum across every finite original
proof generated by the complete presentation. It is infinity exactly when
that grammar has no finite derivation of j.

PROOF. Lemma 4 compares each arbitrary proof with the height-m family.
Lemma 3 constructs an attainer when d_m(j) is finite. If d_m(j) is infinite,
the existence of any finite proof would contradict these lemmas.

The m rounds in the recovered implementation therefore come from a
normalization theorem. They do not truncate the admitted proof family.

To obtain a decreasing policy, define

  rank(j)=the first h with d_h(j)=L(j).

At that first height choose a minimizing rule. Its previous-round child
costs are at least their final minima. Hence

  L(j)
    = c(r)+sum_i d_(rank(j)-1)(p(r,i))
    >= c(r)+sum_i L(p(r,i))
    >= L(j).

All inequalities are equalities. Every child minimum was attained earlier,
so each child rank is strictly smaller. The chosen rule is tight. This
constructs exactly the policy used in Theorem 2.

The solver also records every tight rule, including cyclic ties. A separate
certificate checker verifies all local lower bounds, every retained tight
rule, the selected ranked proof DAG and the unproductive-state certificate.
It does not rerun the solver. For a dead head, every rule must require a dead
premise; structural induction then proves that no finite proof uses that head.
Thus restricting to productive states preserves every original finite proof
of a productive conclusion.

The ordinary numerical fixed-point equation alone needs care. For
x=min(x,1), zero is a numerical solution even though the only grounded proofs
have minimum one. Iteration (8) starts from the empty proof family, represented
by infinity, and obtains one. In ordinary numerical order this is the
greatest fixed point. In reversed tropical information order it is the least.

If the finite input charges are rational, multiply them by the product of
their denominators to obtain an equivalent integer grading. This is a
finite explicit construction preserving comparisons and minimizers.

6. CONCRETE EXECUTION, INCLUDING AN INFINITE SYMBOLIC FAMILY

The kernel-checked symbolic example has a judgement for every n:Nat:

  base proves 0, charge 0;
  step(n) takes n to n+1, charge 1;
  expensive(n) proves n directly, charge n+5;
  loop(n) takes n to n, charge 0.

Set V(n)=n. The first, second and fourth rule schemas are tight; the third
has slack five. The policy chooses base at zero and step at n+1, with
rank(n)=n. Thus it constructs a proof for every n and proves its minimum
grade is n. The theorem chain_all_inputs quantifies over every n and every
competing derivation. Evaluating n=25 returns 25. The separately checked
chainLoop_is_also_minimum retains the looped alternative as another minimum.

This example has infinitely many judgement instances and infinitely many
tied proofs at each judgement. The policy is a finite symbolic definition.

The executable finite cyclic example has

  A <- seed_A, charge 2;       A <- B, charge 0;
  B <- A, charge 0;            B <- seed_B, charge 3;
  C <- A,B, charge 1;          C <- direct, charge 6;
  Z <- Z, charge 0.

Its output is

  A: grade 2, rank 1, selected seed_A;
  B: grade 2, rank 2, selected from_A;
  C: grade 5, rank 3, selected pair(A,B);
  Z: no finite derivation in this supplied grammar.

The A/B zero-cost cycle remains in the complete minimum grammar. Arbitrarily
many finite traversals preserve minimum grade. The ranked selected witness
still terminates. These are exact consequences of the same rules.

A second check has a premise of grade 2/5 occurring twice under a rule of
charge 1/10. Its cost is exactly 9/10. This checks that shared storage never
silently deletes an original premise charge. The independent verifier rejects
a false grade, a discarded tied rule, an unsupported circular proof and an
inexact floating-point charge.

7. INSTITUTIONAL, DEPENDENT AND FRAME RETURN

An institution comorphism (Phi,alpha,beta) satisfies

  N |= alpha(phi) iff beta(N) |= phi.

For Hilbert 24, also retain its actual proof-bearing maps on the exact typed
image and the cost comparison. Let

  E_g:P(j)->N_g(j),   D_g:N_g(j)->P(j),
  D_g E_g=id,         E_g D_g=id,
  W_g(E_g p)=W(p),    W(D_g n)=W_g(n).

Then E_g and D_g restrict to inverse maps

  Min(P(j),W) ~= Min(N_g(j),W_g).                         (9)

PROOF. For native minimum n and arbitrary original competitor p,

  W(D_g n)=W_g(n)<=W_g(E_g p)=W(p).

The reverse direction is identical. The original inverse equations
restrict unchanged. This proof compares all original competitors.
It also works at each fixed grade, giving an equivalence of (7).

Across frames, C_hg=E_h D_g satisfies C_kh C_hg=C_kg.
For an actual typed source operation f, its native operation is E_h f D_g,
and inverse return recovers f exactly.

To compute natively, transport each original constructor separately:

  r_g(n_1,...,n_k)
    = E_(g,h(r)) [r(D_(g,p_1)n_1,...,D_(g,p_k)n_k)].

The same recursion (3) applies these native constructors directly.
Structural induction proves that decoding its result equals the source
constructor (3). No completed source proof is supplied to that recursion.

Dependent sums and products here are the same operations retained by
Sigma_q left-adjoint q* left-adjoint Pi_q. The sum retains the rule/witness
and its mark; the product retains the actual premise section. On a pullback
square the Beck–Chevalley comparison reindexes those same marks. A compatible
family of local minima descends only with the required overlap data; a
selected point additionally retains its policy. No arbitrary global choice
of minimizers is invoked by these transports.

With transported dagger satisfying E_g^dagger=D_g on the exact image,

  C_hg=E_h E_g^dagger,
  C_hg^dagger=C_gh,
  C_gh C_hg=id on N_g(j).

Thus both the minimum family and its comparison arrows have simultaneous
dagger presentations. This is an assertion about the specified equivalences,
not an inference from real eigenvalues of an arbitrary operator.

8. SPARSE MATRIX NORMALIZATION AND THE TERMINAL COALGEBRA

The recovered six-coordinate linear chart is

  rho(t,b,B) =
    [[t-2b, 0,    0,    0],
     [0,    B11,  B12,  0],
     [0,    B21,  B22,  0],
     [b,    0,    0,    t]].

Its independent coordinates are t,b and the four entries of B. Read them
from the bottom-right, bottom-left and central block. The generic chart has
seven potentially occupied positions, with the top-left entry dependent.
Six independent coefficients should not be confused with six possible
nonzero matrix entries in every linear combination.

One integral Gram form on this image is

  H = [[1,0,0,1], [0,2,-1,0], [0,-1,2,0], [1,0,0,2]],
  det(H)=3,
  D(M)=H^(-1) M^T H.

The central block is the recovered A2 Gram form. On the outer block the
identity H_2 A=A^T H_2 holds for A=[[t-2b,0],[b,t]]. Consequently D preserves
the sparse image, and D^2=id. All its inverse denominators are odd.

For this additive involution,

  N_D=-(I+2D)/3,
  (I-2D)N_D=N_D(I-2D)=I.

For the six-strand rotation U with U^6=I, transported to the sparse matrix
image by rho U delta,

  N_U=-(1/63) sum_(j=0)^5 2^j U^j,
  (I-2U)N_U=N_U(I-2U)=I.                                 (10)

PROOF. Multiply the finite geometric sum:

  (I-2U) sum_(j=0)^5 2^j U^j=I-64U^6=-63I.

This is an exact identity over Z_2. It holds at every dyadic residue level
and on the inverse limit; there is no precision cutoff. U here is a module
endomorphism on the six coordinates, not an asserted conjugation by a
four-by-four matrix.

Both I-2U and its inverse preserve the integral lattice. Each is
nonexpansive in the coefficient sup ultrametric, so together they imply
N_U is an isometry. The same argument applies to N_D. Thus normalization
preserves the exact cylinder relation x=y mod 2^m for every m.

If E_g,D_g are the proof-bearing chart maps, the normalized chart is

  E_g^N=N_U E_g,
  D_g^N=D_g(I-2U).

Their inverse equations follow from (10). Pull back the original grade
along D_g^N. Theorem (9) then applies to the normalized native representation.
Retain proof tags and typed constructor actions throughout: signed ring
coefficients alone need not preserve tropical support, because leading
terms can cancel. The needed comparison is the exact proof/grade return.

The base-3/2 carry

  (a,b)->(a-3q,b+2q)

preserves a+(3/2)b in Q_2, or equivalently the integral expression 2a+3b.
With q retained, the inverse is (a',b')->(a'+3q,b'-2q). This is a reversible
coefficient operation available to the native constructors.

The supplied carrier C~=({0,1}^6)^Nat is the terminal coalgebra for
F(X)={0,1}^6 x X. Its parity/continuation map retains complete six-channel
behavior. The proof family P=mu R and the minimum family mu R_tight are
inductive. The terminal carrier can store and execute the finite proof
programs, and exact complete-stream results can be sound constructor macros.
The policy theorem gives their original finite proof return when those typed
constructors are supplied. No finite proof-depth approximation to the
terminal coalgebra is required.

For a marked inverse Collatz block

  B_w(y)=(2^k y-A_w)/3^s,

the exact valuation identity is

  v_2(B_w(y)-B_w(y'))=k+v_2(y-y').

The strong triangle inequality permits reuse of one sufficiently precise
tail across many returns without adding an error for each use. That precision
comparison coexists with the separate proof-grade identity (2).

9. COST AND SOURCE COVERAGE

In a finite presentation with m judgements, R rules and A ordered premise
incidences, the elementary recovered fold performs O(m(R+A)) exact arithmetic
steps. A finite common denominator and the cycle-erasure tree-size bound keep
the relevant rational bit lengths polynomial in the explicit presentation.
The chosen proof can be retained as a ranked DAG. Its expanded tree can be
larger, and writing that expansion has its own output cost.

This statement measures construction in the complete presentation supplied.
The grade in (1) is the declared proof-tree occurrence objective. Complete
machine execution time includes obtaining rule data, doing arithmetic,
constructing and checking the packet, and returning its chosen output format.
A sharing-aware execution objective must put the available resource state
and its charges into the original calculus.

There are two precise ways to establish global original optimality:

  A. A complete grade-preserving proof equivalence represents every original
     competitor, and native minimization transports through it.
  B. Calibrate every original inference rule directly, then return one native
     construction whose decoded original proof attains the source potential.

Method B needs no enumeration or surjection from a search chart onto all
competitors. The universal original lower bound already supplies the
comparison. The current work establishes this constructive H24 mechanism.
It makes no new source-specific proof or complexity claim for P, Hodge, Tate,
BSD or Yang–Mills.
