12. Proof-relevant partition folds, tropical minima, and six-frame Haar

Complete original source: Hilbert_24_Collected_Proofs.zip :: Proofs/Hilbert24_Exact_Returns/Hilbert24_Exact_Returns/tesseract_path_integral/papers_a/Proof_Partition_Theorem.txt

A finite acyclic proof grammar with ordered smaller-index premises, natural rule prices and positive rational weights. All premise occurrences and original rule labels are retained. Conditional normalization requires a nonempty accepted fibre; quantum and thermal expressions use the specified normalized positive measure. Haar uses the declared frame-product measure and compatible cylinder integrals. Diagram-to-proof returns are restricted to the stated typed correct-net or e-hypergraph interfaces and their cited realization conditions; pushed-forward mass is retained when semantic alternatives are identified.

§1 Complete minimum-proof subgrammar; §2 Haar and accepted-mass normalization; §3 Tropical/free-energy bounds and quantum expectation; §4 Three-reflection Haar identity; §5 Typed e-hypergraph and proof-net interfaces

PROOF-RELEVANT PARTITION FOLDS, TROPICAL MINIMA, AND SIX-FRAME HAAR

This theorem is a new composition of the recovered proof-grammar minimum
construction with a weighted sum/product interpretation. Published inputs
are identified in Sources_A.json. The returned rule DAG retains the actual
ordered premises and every tight alternative.

1. FINITE CONSTRUCTOR, WHOLE PROOF FAMILY

Fix a finite acyclic proof grammar. Each rule r has head h, ordered premises
c_1,...,c_k<h, natural price c_r, and positive rational weight w_r. The
constructive family of proofs of h is the disjoint sum, over rules r at h,
of the products of the child proof families. Its price is the rule price
plus the prices of its actual premise occurrences. Its probability mass
is the rule weight times the product of child masses.

The partition circuit is constructed by the topological fold

 Z_h(q) = sum_(r:head(r)=h) w_r q^(c_r) product_i Z_(c_i)(q).

The empty sum is zero and the empty product is one. At every inhabited
vertex define d_h as its minimum exponent and m_h as the coefficient of
q^(d_h). Since coefficients are nonnegative, the same fold gives

 d_h = min_r [c_r + sum_i d_(c_i)],
 m_h = sum_(tight r) w_r product_i m_(c_i).

The minimum is absent for an empty proof fibre. A rule is tight when its
displayed candidate equals d_h. Retain every tight rule, with its original
ordered premises. Induction shows that this retained subgrammar generates
every and only minimum source proof. Selecting the first tight rule at
each visited vertex constructs one attainer; retaining all rules describes
the complete fibre. Repeated premise occurrences are charged separately.

Proof. The product family adds prices and multiplies weights. The disjoint
sum adds masses and selects the smallest exponent. Positive coefficients
make the coefficient at that exponent exactly the displayed tight-rule sum.
This is an induction on h, so it produces the lower bound and an attaining
backpointer together. The full arithmetic circuit requires one topological
pass over the written rules and premises. Expanded polynomials and exact
large rational values are charged by the arithmetic actually performed.

For the previously recovered fibre with minimum grade4 and generating
polynomial 6+q^2, the normalized full partition is

 Z(q)=q^4(6+q^2)/7,
 d=4, m=6/7, and the retained tight grammar has six original witnesses.

2. HAAR NORMALIZATION AND INFINITE CYLINDER FIBRES

Give S3 normalized counting measure and S3^N its product Haar probability.
For a finite frame word g_1,...,g_H, its cylinder has mass6^(-H). A function
depending on the first H frames integrates by its finite transfer fold;
the entire infinite tail contributes the factor1. Refining by one unused
frame preserves the integral because its six conditional masses sum to1.
The compatible cylinder integrals give the canonical integral of locally
constant observables; uniform limits inherit it with the sup-norm bound.

The same operation can be paired with the canonical dyadic cylinder
B_k(a)=a+2^k Z2 of measure2^(-k): use the product measure, of total mass1.
The frame coordinate and the original dyadic point remain distinct fields
of the marked state. The finite observation leaves the full tail fibre.

When every reachable premise fibre is inhabited and its local rule masses
sum to1, induction gives Z_h(1)=1. Empty fibres have Z_h=0; in a grammar
containing failed branches the total mass is the accepted mass. For any
nonempty proof fibre the explicit normalization Z_h(q)/Z_h(1) constructs
its conditional probability partition, with minimum mass m_h/Z_h(1).

3. TROPICAL LIMIT AND LITERAL QUANTUM AMPLITUDE

Let mu be the normalized proof-family measure and C its price. For beta>0,

 Z(e^(-beta)) = integral exp(-beta C(p)) dmu(p).

Let d be the minimum and m>0 its total mass. Then

 m exp(-beta d) <= Z(e^(-beta)) <= exp(-beta d),
 d <= -log(Z(e^(-beta)))/beta <= d-log(m)/beta.

Therefore the free energy tends to d. This is the explicit tropical return
of the same partition circuit; m records the entire minimum fibre's mass.
For a finite grammar Z(e^(-z)) is an entire function of complex z.

On the Hilbert space with orthonormal basis indexed by the original proofs,
set Omega_p=sqrt(mu(p)) and C_hat|p>=C(p)|p>. Then

 Z(e^(-it)) = <Omega, exp(-it C_hat) Omega>.

This is a literal unitary expectation, with ||Omega||=1. Its compact
calculation is the same source-DAG fold. The probability coefficient is
retained when passing to the complex-time evaluation.

For frame walks with F,T involutions, put P=(F+T)/2. A separate heat-time
parameter t gives the normalized Poisson path integral

 exp(-2t) sum_(n>=0) (2t)^n/n! P^n = exp[-t(2I-F-T)].

Each P^n is the transfer fold of n binary frame steps; the Poisson weights
sum to1. This connects the finite path constructor to the parent's entire
six-coordinate frame heat operator. Inverse temperature beta, quantum
time, and Poisson heat time have their distinct displayed roles.

4. THREE BINARY REFLECTIONS CONSTRUCT SIX-FRAME HAAR

Let s,t be two generating transpositions of S3. The exact group-ring identity

 ((1+s)/2)((1+2t)/3)((1+s)/2)
    = (1+s+t+st+ts+sts)/6

follows by expansion, s^2=1, and the six S3 normal forms. It is the Haar
element. Its square equals itself, since left multiplication permutes the
six group elements, and its augmentation equals1. Applying any representation
gives the corresponding normalized invariant projector. The construction
is the three-stage distribution in Hall-Puder-Sawin, formula(1.4).

5. TYPED E-HYPERGRAPH AND PROOF-NET INTERFACES

Theorem VI.8 of Tiurin-Barrett-Ghica-Hu identifies the free semilattice-
enriched symmetric monoidal theory modulo its equations with the EDPOI
e-hypergraph presentation. Its objects have fixed input/output interfaces;
rewrite matches are convex with the stated boundary complement. The
interpretation takes a generator to its labelled hyperedge, composition
to gluing of matching interfaces, tensor to disjoint juxtaposition, and
join to a hierarchical alternative edge. The inverse reads a directed
acyclic diagram in topological order, retaining its equivalence witnesses.

For the part encoded as typed unit-free MLL proof structures, use the
Danos-Regnier switching correctness certificate. Di Guardia-Laurent-
Tortora de Falco-Vaux Auclair, Theorem6.1, constructs a sequent derivation
by splitting-vertex induction. Theorem6.18 preserves a closed interface;
Theorem6.19 gives a mix-free derivation for a connected net. Lemma5.5 says
that proof substitution becomes interface gluing after desequentialization.

Thus a typed correct-net representative selected by the tight grammar has
an actual source sequent return. Rule occurrences and their prices are
retained in the grammar, so the Hilbert24 certificate follows the return.

Semilattice joins identify repeated semantic alternatives. For probability
or amplitude integration, retain the pushed-forward mass of the original
marked proofs at each identified alternative. Its graded coefficient is
the sum of those masses at each price. This is the measure-preserving
interface between equivalence saturation and the partition circuit.
13. Triple adjoints, exact institution returns, and complete original minima

Complete original source: Hilbert_24_Collected_Proofs.zip :: Proofs/Hilbert24_Exact_Returns/Hilbert24_Exact_Returns/institutions/Adjoint_and_Institution_Proofs.txt

The adjoint construction uses actual set-valued families with extensional function equality; higher path-valued interpretations retain the required equality paths and transport. Institution comorphisms have the displayed satisfaction condition, with source-model coverage separately required for reflection. Proof return requires actual rule translation and inverse on the specified marked image. Complete minimum transport uses inverse witness codecs and original cost pulled through the decoder. Uniform execution bounds retain an actual producer, code-size relations and the costs of decoding, production, encoding, checking and output.

§1 Sigma_q ⊣ q* ⊣ Pi_q; §2 Beck–Chevalley and pasting; §3 Signed evidence-rail return; §4 Institution comorphism and proof-rule transport; §5 Constructed normalized primitive proof; §6 Complete argmin-fibre equivalence; §7 Literal producer and total operation bound; §8 Datatype coverage

TRIPLE ADJOINTS, EXACT INSTITUTION RETURNS, AND THE ORIGINAL MINIMUM

1. CONSTRUCT THE THREE FUNCTORS ON THE ACTUAL FIBRES

For a function q:X->Y and a family P over X, define

  (Sigma_q P)(y) = {(x,w): q(x)=y and w in P(x)},
  (q* A)(x) = A(q(x)),
  (Pi_q P)(y) = {s: for every x with q(x)=y, s(x) in P(x)}.

The source label x is part of a dependent-sum witness. A dependent-product
witness is a section, not a Boolean claim that a section exists. The maps
below manipulate actual marked pairs or actual functions.

Given h:Sigma_q P -> A, its transpose is

  h_hat(x,w)=h(q(x),(x,w)).

Given k:P -> q* A, its inverse transpose is

  k_check(y,(x,w))=k(x,w), with q(x)=y.

Substitution proves both composites identity. This is the complete hom-set
bijection Sigma_q left-adjoint q*. The unit inserts (x,w); the counit
evaluates the original marked witness in the original y-fibre. The triangle
identities reduce to pair construction followed by its original projection.

Given k:q* A -> P, define

  k_hat(y,a)(x)=k(x,a) for q(x)=y.

Given h:A -> Pi_q P, recover k(x,a)=h(q(x),a)(x). Again both composites
are identities, pointwise for the returned sections. This proves q*
left-adjoint Pi_q. The product transpose constructs a lazy function from
the given map; it does not select an unknown member from each fibre.

Thus Sigma_q --| q* --| Pi_q is implemented by exact forward and inverse
maps. The universal proof is the preceding substitution argument. The 421
executable checks in adjoint_fibres_checks.json exercise the same maps on
finite examples, including empty fibres, both evidence rails, pasted
pullbacks and exact object retention. Finite checks are not substituted
for the universal argument.

This chapter uses set-valued families and extensional equality of functions.
In a path/groupoid-valued setting, the pullback also carries the equality
path and the corresponding transport; it must not be erased. The current
code asserts the displayed set-level theorem, while retaining the typed
places where the higher path data enter.

Background: Lawvere, Quantifiers and Sheaves (1970),
https://lawverearchives.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/1970-quantifiers-and-sheaves.pdf.

2. BECK--CHEVALLEY WITH THE WITNESS KEPT, INCLUDING PASTING

Given f:Z->Y, form the actual pullback

  X'={(z,x): f(z)=q(x)},  q'(z,x)=z,  g(z,x)=x.

The sum comparison f*Sigma_q P -> Sigma_q' g*P sends

  (z,(x,w)) -> ((z,x),w).

Its inverse sends ((z,x),w) to (z,(x,w)). Both are defined on exactly the
displayed compatibility equation f(z)=q(x). The very same w is returned;
the implementation even checks identity of supplied witness objects.

The product comparison f*Pi_q P -> Pi_q' g*P sends a section s to

  s'((z,x))=s(x),

and its inverse sends s' to the function x -> s'((z,x)). Their composites
are pointwise identity. No finite approximation to an entire section is
used by this construction.

For another base map h:W->Z, the iterated pullback has points
(w,(h(w),x)); the direct pullback has (w,x). The comparison deletes the
redundant middle coordinate and its inverse reinserts h(w). Marked sums
retain w_evidence unchanged. Product sections precompose with these same
inverse point maps. Substitution proves equality of the direct and pasted
routes. This is the coherence needed for a metagraph whose paths must
return the original point, rather than just an isomorphic-looking type.

3. BOTH BELNAP RAILS TRAVEL THROUGH THE ADJOINTS

Represent finite observable evidence as (support,opposition). For a family,

  exists = (OR support, AND opposition),
  forall = (AND support, OR opposition),
  negation swaps the two coordinates.

At the proof-object level, an existential packet contains a positive
marked witness and an opposing section. A universal packet contains a
positive section and an opposing marked witness. Consequently existential
base change uses Sigma on the positive rail and Pi on the opposing rail;
universal base change uses Pi and Sigma respectively. The two comparisons
from section 2 supply the actual maps and their inverses on both rails.

Negation exchanges these packets exactly and commutes with the comparison.
The four observable values remain distinguishable: neither, positive only,
opposing only, and both. No identification with a single classical truth bit
is silently introduced. Nothing here invokes a choice principle or uses
Diaconescu's theorem to replace this evidence semantics by excluded middle.

4. THE INSTITUTION COMORPHISM AND ITS EXACT DIRECTION

An institution has signatures, sentences, models and satisfaction, with
truth invariant under signature translation. For a comorphism from I to J,
use the convention

  Phi: Sign_I -> Sign_J,
  alpha_Sigma: Sen_I(Sigma) -> Sen_J(Phi Sigma),
  beta_Sigma: Mod_J(Phi Sigma) -> Mod_I(Sigma),

and the satisfaction equation

  M' satisfies_J alpha(phi) iff beta(M') satisfies_I phi.

The original signed version applies this equation separately to support
and opposition. Composing two comorphisms composes alpha forward and beta
backward. Naturality follows componentwise; the identity and associativity
laws are ordinary substitution of these component maps.

For semantic reflection over every source model, each source model must
have an appropriate target-model lift (or a specified covering equivalence).
The source model-return/cover hypotheses remain explicit. Without them a
translation can preserve validity on the models it sees without covering
all original models. The original formal file retains this distinction.

For proof objects, add a translation of each rule and a proof decoder on
the specified image. An induction on the actual proof tree proves

  Gamma |- phi  -->  alpha(Gamma) |- alpha(phi).

At an assumption leaf the same source assumption is translated; at an
inference node the translated rule consumes the recursively translated
children. If each node has an exact inverse on the marked image, induction
also proves the whole proof and context return. No arbitrary target proof
is assumed decodable outside that declared image.

The unchanged Institution_Comorphism_Core.het has 22 freshly proved
first-order goals. Those component laws do not by themselves assert every
coherence law of an unrestricted higher category of institutions. The
record-level formulas above state the actual categorical construction, and
the next section instantiates proof translation on a genuine constructed
source derivation rather than a placeholder proposition.

Primary institution reference: Goguen and Rosu, Institution Morphisms,
https://ntrs.nasa.gov/citations/20010097127.

5. A CONSTRUCTED STOKES PRIMITIVE, NOT AN ASSUMED TARGET WITNESS

The retained proof kernel uses an unchanged eight-schema cubical interface:
the contraction

  dK+Kd=I-P,

and d^2=0, K^2=0, Pd=0, PK=0, together with the required zero and addition
identities. For an arbitrary cochain A, construct

  B=K(dA).

Apply the contraction to dA:

  dK(dA)+K(d^2A)=dA-P(dA).

The source zero laws reduce this to dB=dA. The remaining equations are
KB=K^2(dA)=0 and PB=PK(dA)=0. Pair B with these three equality proofs,
then abstract over the arbitrary A. The result is the actual dependent
proof term

  Pi A. Sigma B. (dB=dA AND KB=0 AND PB=0).

This is a lambda proof over A, not a sample table. The retained original
proof kernel validates all 26 rule occurrences. Three injective symbol-and-
sort recodings, named folded, stokes and matrix, preserve and recheck this
same formal derivation. This runtime replay establishes exact structural
recoding of the proof and context; concrete operator interpretations need
their separately supplied intertwining maps. The native view retains the
complete source derivation as a payload alongside its six-coordinate mark.
Its inverse returns the source proof and source context byte-for-byte;
each of the six native frames reads the same retained derivation. The receipt is
constructive_transport_receipt.json:

  source rule occurrences:       26
  translated rule occurrences:   26
  added source axioms:             0
  deleted source axioms:           0
  inverse context equality:    true
  exact proof readback:        true.

Wrong-rail and altered-charge packets are rejected. The 26-count measures
source inference occurrences; it is not a claim of zero machine time for
encoding, verification or decoding. The eight source contraction laws are
still present. Zero axiom deviation means exactly that this original
context was neither enlarged nor erased during transport. The underlying
source CASL file labels its contraction laws as %implied goals. This
26-step replay receives those laws as an interface; it does not independently
reprove the unrestricted source contraction goals. A separate concrete
native matrix model and its actual intertwiners are supplied in
Concrete_Native_Stokes_Proof.txt.

The Python runtime labels its closed demonstrated targets explicitly.
Binding a different full programme target requires its actual original
Hets declaration and a checked source derivation for that declaration.
This primitive theorem is not relabelled as an unprovided proof of a
Millennium problem.

6. THE ORIGINAL MINIMUM TRANSPORTS WITH ALL COMPETITORS

Let W_x be the complete admissible source witness fibre, with source cost
kappa_x. Let E_x:W_x -> W'_Ex and D_x be inverse maps on the specified
target fibre, and define the target cost by

  kappa'_Ex(w')=kappa_x(D_x(w')).

Suppose w_min belongs to W_x and the source proof establishes

  for every v in W_x, kappa_x(w_min) <= kappa_x(v).

For any target competitor v', the decoder supplies the source competitor
D_x(v'). Applying the source inequality proves

  kappa'_Ex(E_x(w_min)) <= kappa'_Ex(v').

The inverse argument proves the converse. Thus the minima and entire
argmin fibres correspond exactly, not only their numerical infima. The
decoded competitor must range over the original admissible fibre; a
restricted approximation cannot establish the full original minimum.

Literal implementation from adjoint_fibres.py:

  def transported_cost(codec, original_cost):
      """Pull back the ORIGINAL charge. Encoding work is a separate account."""
      return lambda encoded: original_cost(codec.decode(encoded))

  def transport_lower_certificate(codec, lower):
      """Carry an existing all-competitor certificate by exact decoding."""
      return lambda encoded_competitor: lower(codec.decode(encoded_competitor))

There is an actual closed minimum in the companion aligned Fredkin--Toffoli
problem. Its six group elements have shortest words of lengths
0,1,1,2,2,3. The Cayley graph is a six-cycle. Graph distance is an attained
lower bound against every word in these two fixed generators. Reading a
word once computes its exact element, then the six-state reader supplies
its shortest representative. The same bound returns through the point
labelling, native matrix and analytic charts. This is a concrete
Hilbert-24-style simplicity result for the displayed presentation.

The residual-energy minimum is separately attained by the original
orthogonal projection E: for Eu=u,

  ||f-u||^2=||(1-E)f||^2+||Ef-u||^2.

Therefore Ef attains the original coarse-family minimum, with every
competitor represented. The retained detail (1-E)f is needed to return f.
These two proved minima have their original domains and charges; their
existence alone does not construct a uniform optimizer for another domain.

7. THE UNIFORM PRODUCER IS A LITERAL SOURCE ARGUMENT

The original HPOProducerData declaration is extracted verbatim in
sources/Original_HPO_Producer.hetpart with its master and window hashes.
It defines

  hpoDeadline(c,d,f)=c*(1+cnInputBits(f))^d

and quantifies over an actual machine m and constants c,d. Its premise
HPOUniformCalibrated(m,c,d) requires that the same deterministic machine
acquire the actual tight original receipt for every original 3CNF within
that clock. The return theorem is the literal implication

  HPOUniformCalibrated(m,c,d) => HPOUniformShortest(m,c,d).

Thus this particular premise comes from the original source definition of
a uniform calibrated producer. An attained polynomial minimum in a
specified ultrametric space would discharge it only after constructing the
required m,c,d and proving the all-input acquisition bound for the very
same verifier and source formula. The recovered implication itself does
not derive that acquisition claim from an unnamed minimum theorem.

For an actually supplied producer p, input codec Ex/Dx and witness codec
Ew/Dw, the executable transported producer is literally

  def transport_producer(input_codec, witness_codec, producer):
      """Exact implementation of E_w o producer o D_x. No bound is inferred."""
      return lambda input: witness_codec.encode(producer(input_codec.decode(input)))

The full operation bound is

  T_transport(z)=T_Dx(z)+T_p(Dx(z))+T_Ew(p(Dx(z))),

plus any explicitly requested verification and output-writing cost.
Polynomial bounds are preserved when the actual component bounds and size
relations are supplied. This explains precisely why the uniform producer
and bound remain arguments of this transport theorem.

8. FORMAL REPLAY AND DATATYPE COVERAGE

The triple-adjoint source was recovered as fourteen literal spec blocks.
It contains 34 named goals. The final clean-premise replay proves all 34:

  27 exact original FOF conjectures with E refutations;
   7 constructor-elimination proofs from the original CASL free types,
     with the seven complete cases checked by E.

All retained FOF premises are byte-identical original exported formulas.
All inherited goal names are removed from the premise sets. No source
axiom is added and no original source formula is edited.

For a sole nonrecursive free constructor D::=c(A;B), source CASL semantics
gives the exact elimination rule

  (forall d:D. Phi(d)) iff (forall a:A,b:B. Phi(c(a,b))).

Seven original FOF exports omitted this datatype coverage while retaining
selectors and injectivity. Rather than adding a reconstruction assumption,
the replay proves the constructor cases and uses the source elimination
rule. The detailed seven-case coverage argument and input/proof hashes
are in adjoint_verification/focused/Datatype_Translation_Proof.txt and
combined_validation.json. Baseline unsuccessful logs remain distinguishable
from this final result.

The automated prover is classical first-order E. Its proof objects certify
those exported consequences. The pair/function constructions, datatype
elimination and actual Stokes proof term provide the constructive arguments
stated here; a classical ATP receipt is not described as a general
constructive proof translation of every source logic.
14. Dependency support of actual original return proofs

Complete original source: Hilbert24_Executable.zip :: Hilbert24_Executable/proof_reduction/Proof_Support_Reduction.proof.txt

A supplied finite original TPTP premise environment and an actual saved proof DAG of the same original conjecture. The reduction retains the ancestors and exact original input-leaf formulas, roles, names, source scopes and evidence lanes. Induction applies each unchanged inference to its retained parents. Iterated strict premise deletion terminates by decreasing finite context size, while ATP discovery cost between rounds remains separately charged. The theorem establishes the support of the supplied/replayed proof; it does not assert logical independence or globally fewest premises.

§2 Proof-support theorem; §3 Strict-deletion termination; §4 Original institution and torser source return

HILBERT 24 ON THE ACTUAL RETURN PROOFS: DEPENDENCY SUPPORT
21 November 2026

1. The original problem stays fixed

Let Gamma be the finite set of ORIGINAL TPTP premises exported for one
selected Hets conclusion phi. An original proof DAG pi of Gamma |- phi is
already supplied. In the exported refutation presentation its leaves are
original axioms together with the assumption-negation transformation of
the same original conjecture. The final node is the empty clause.

The transformation reads pi itself. It does not search subsets of Gamma,
enumerate mathematical inputs, or decide the original full target. It
computes the ancestors of the final proof node and collects their original
input leaves. Let U(pi) be the set of axiom leaves obtained in this way.
The conjecture leaf is checked to be the unique original conjecture.

The new input is the exact ordered subsequence U(pi) of original premises,
with every original formula's text retained byte-for-byte. The original
conjecture is retained byte-for-byte too. Only unused original axiom spans
are deleted from the file; the explicit deletion certificate records each
span and its hash. No new endpoint, sibling theorem or helper assumption
is inserted.

2. The proof-support theorem

Every node in the retained ancestor DAG is derivable from U(pi), using
the same inference as in pi. Prove this by induction in its finite
topological order. An axiom leaf belongs to U(pi) by construction. A
conjecture-negation leaf is the same original refutation hypothesis.
For an internal node all its parents are earlier ancestors, hence their
derivations have already been returned; apply the original inference.
The final empty clause returns the same original conjecture.

Thus pi itself supplies a proof of U(pi) |- phi. This is a concrete
proof-preserving weakening of the premise context. It requires neither
choice of a source witness nor a test over all models of Gamma.

After parsing, ancestor traversal takes time linear in the number of DAG
nodes and reference edges, under the usual graph-operation cost model.
Parsing, textual copying, hashing and certificate checking are additional
work and are recorded separately. The implementation does not claim a
linear bit bound for every operation of Python's parser.

3. Replaying and reaching a dependency fixed point

The transformation submits the reduced exact problem to E. A new
successful proof pi_1 can use a still smaller support. The process repeats
only when it can delete at least one further premise. It stops when all
remaining premises occur in the returned proof DAG.

The natural-number measure |Gamma| strictly decreases on every deletion
round. There can therefore be at most |Gamma| such rounds. This is a
termination proof for dependency deletion, not a uniform time bound for
the ATP search between rounds. The supplied proof already justifies the
deletion; a fresh search gives an additional reproducible prover result.

At termination the remaining context equals the support of the returned
proof. This is the precise meaning of proof-support fixed point. It does
not assert that each retained premise is logically independent, that no
other proof uses fewer premises, or that the returned proof is globally
shortest. Those are different comparisons requiring different evidence.

4. Institutional and torser source conditions survive

The local Hets conclusions connect original source operations with their
retained torser charts, dagger/native matrix and complete target readers.
Their mathematical assumptions remain distinguished from generated
signature/definedness assumptions by the exact original premise audits.

The three previously strengthened possibly-empty-source inputs are used
as the starting inputs. The deleted spurious defined-inhabitant formula
is absent at the beginning and cannot return: the new file is a literal
subsequence of the starting file. Partial tag reads, definedness and any
remaining typing premises are retained exactly when they occur in the
proof support. Dropping an unused premise yields a stronger conditional
theorem; it does not assume an inhabitant of an original empty sort.

Likewise, the Hodge return still concerns the original cycle/class; the
BSD return still carries its actual degree correction and height entries;
the RH witness return still uses the original marked form; the SAT return
keeps all original refuter challenges; the Piola return keeps the actual
metric/density; and the Yang--Mills spectral return keeps the same source
Hamiltonian/domain conditions. Premise reduction changes the sufficient
support for those local conclusions. It does not close a full problem
endpoint whose original source supplier is still an obligation.

5. Checking boundary and search observations

The verifier checks exact surviving formula bytes, unchanged conjecture,
original order, recorded deletion spans, dependency closure, all referenced
input labels, and the final fixed-point support. The E proof object and
fresh Theorem outcome are retained. The verifier is a structural certificate
checker and proof-input auditor, not a newly implemented independent
first-order inference kernel.

The actual runs show why Hilbert24 must keep separate cost coordinates.
Most reduced problems replayed immediately under E's ordinary automatic
strategy. Two smaller problems changed that heuristic's behaviour and hit
its resource limit. The documented scheduling mode then proved both.
Search_Observations.json records those failed discovery attempts and the
successful scheduling runs. Fewer premises is an exact reduction of this
declared support metric; it is not an automatic runtime improvement.

The original proof-source bytes and their comments are included. Formula
identity and the actual ancestor DAG carry the comparison, so changing a
printed theory name or discarding a quantitative source field is not a
permitted simplification. The helper's guard tests reject changed formulas,
missing conjectures, inserted assumptions, forged deletion lists and
unregistered proof leaves.

6. Executable entry points

python h24_support.py reduce --source-bundle OLD_BUNDLE --proof-dir OLD_LOGS
    --out RESULTS --eprover PATH_TO_E

python h24_support.py verify --out RESULTS

Reduction_Receipt.json contains all job-specific counts, support names,
input/proof hashes and the exact scope. Deletion_Verification.json records
the independent exact-byte deletion replay over all completed jobs. The
recorded sources, proof DAGs, reduced inputs and final proofs make these
comparisons reproducible without rebuilding the old canonical archive.
15. Hilbert 24 with the native CRT and Čech return

Complete original source: Hilbert_24_Native_CRT_Cech.zip :: Hilbert_24_Native_CRT_Cech/README.md

finite original CNF and supplied original clause order; original clause indices and variable labels retained frontier w=max_i|F_i|; word input length N CRT moduli coprime 2^k and 5^ell with product strictly greater than 2^n valuation read requires 2h>d where d is unread original bit count native Gram determinant 3 is a unit for full dagger; integral Gram relation otherwise

marked least-action/count/witness alternative and serial rules; frontier row lower bound and attainment; dyadic leading-valuation minimum read; original all-degree Čech contraction; 2^w poly(N) bit-work theorem

# Hilbert 24 with the native CRT and Čech return

## The common marked operator

Let \(C\) be the original CNF. For each labelled original assignment \(a\), take one copy of the original native fibre with Gram matrix
\[
G=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&1\\0&2&-1&0\\0&-1&2&0\\1&0&0&2\end{pmatrix}.
\]
A clause \(i\) has failure idempotent \(P_i(a)=[a\text{ fails }i]I_4\), with each original clause index retained. Put \(A_C(a)=|\{i:a\text{ fails }i\}|\). The marked operator and its scalar partition are
\[
 B_C(t)|_a=\prod_i\big(I_4+(t-1)P_i(a)\big)=t^{A_C(a)}I_4,\qquad
 Z_C(t)=\sum_a (B_C(t)|_a)_{44}.
\]
Every factor is self-adjoint for \(G\) over \(\mathbb Z[1/3]\) and satisfies the integral Gram equation over any coefficient ring. Scalar factors commute with all six native frame operations. Take one native coordinate per original assignment when forming \(Z_C\). The full ordinary trace would introduce a factor of four.

In the Cayley presentation let \(S_j\) shift the Boolean label in coordinate \(j\). On the Walsh mode marked by \(a\), it has eigenvalue \((-1)^{a_j}\). Where two is a unit, \((I+(-1)^b S_j)/2\) projects onto \(a_j=b\). Multiplying these projections for the original falsifying literals gives \(P_i\) in the Walsh presentation. At a dyadic cut, take the same original Boolean values through the integral idempotents. The original clause and assignment marks identify the two presentations.

The four-bit Rule 30 realization
\[
 (\ell,c,r,u)\longmapsto(\ell,c,r,u\oplus\ell\oplus(c\lor r))
\]
is an involution. On the sum of identical native Gram fibres, the induced block permutation is an isometry. Reindex the mark and conjugate its original operator together. This preserves the clause action, joint failure overlaps, and original proof readback.

The same polynomial has these readouts.

| Evaluation | Original return |
| --- | --- |
| \(t=0\) | The original satisfying projector \(S_C(a)=\prod_i(I_4-P_i(a))\) |
| \(t=1/4\) over the reals | Power \(4^{-A_C(a)}\) on each marked assignment, with largest power \(4^{-E_C}\) |
| \(t=4^h\), \(2h>d\) on a marked cylinder with \(d\) unread bits | \(E_C=\lfloor v_2(Z_C(4^h))/(2h)\rfloor\) on that cylinder |
| Least degree with original backpointers | Minimum action \(E_C\), its multiplicity, and one original attaining assignment |

For the dyadic formula, the leading term has valuation \(2hE_C+v_2(M_{E_C})\le 2hE_C+d\) because its positive multiplicity is at most \(2^d\). All higher terms are in \(2^{2h(E_C+1)}\mathbb Z_2\). The strict case of the ultrametric inequality forces the leading valuation. The runtime computes the minimum degree without evaluating the full polynomial or materializing the assignment space.

## The Hilbert 24 rule with CRT and an original mark

A nonempty family of paths has a summary consisting of its least action, the residues of the least-action multiplicity, and an original attaining mark. Over alternatives, compare grades, keep the lower one, and add residues when the grades tie. Keep either attaining mark on a tie in the supplied order. Over independent path extensions, add grades and multiply residues while composing marks. The underlying \(0/1\) selection depends on the branch data and grades. It is independent of whether a multiplicity residue vanishes.

This rule follows by disjoint union of alternatives and Cartesian product of independent choices. A lower-bound proof comes from comparing with every candidate predecessor. An attaining proof comes from the retained predecessor. Residues can be updated independently because the rules for counts use addition and multiplication. For \(n\) original variables the implementation uses coprime moduli \(2^k,5^\ell\) with product \(>2^n\), so the root multiplicity is reconstructed exactly. These two coefficient components support the full Gram dagger since \(\det G=3\). A triadic component also supports the integral Gram relation and marked frame inverses.

## Constructing the ranked derivation from the original clauses

After clause \(i\), let \(F_i\) be the variables which have occurred and will occur again. Index the table by assignments on \(F_i\). A row represents all assignments to the variables seen through \(i\) with those fixed values. Its grade is the least number of failed original clauses through \(i\). Its count records exactly the assignments in that row attaining that grade. The empty initial row has grade \(0\) and multiplicity \(1\).

For a transition, group old rows by their assignment on \(F_{i-1}\cap F_i\) and by whether an old literal makes clause \(i\) true. Merge each group by the alternative rule. All forgotten coordinates in a group have the same influence on the current clause and on all future clauses. A more expensive path in that group can be replaced by the group's attained minimum, with the same continuation. Fixing a new row then fixes the newly introduced persistent literal values.

Let \(U\) contain the variables appearing only in clause \(i\). If a persistent or old literal makes the clause true, all \(2^{|U|}\) assignments on \(U\) have local grade \(0\). Otherwise, if \(U\ne\varnothing\), the first literal on \(U\) supplies an attaining grade-zero choice. The number of such choices is \(2^{|U|}-\epsilon\), with \(\epsilon=0\) if opposite signs on some variable prevent all literals from being false, and \(\epsilon=1\) otherwise. The higher-grade internal alternatives have identical future data. If \(U=\varnothing\) and the clause is still false, there is one grade-one choice.

Add the local grade and multiply the two residue components per group. Merge the resulting alternatives for the new row. This proves the row invariant by induction and gives both lower-bound and attainment evidence. The code uses modular exponentiation for the count on \(U\), with one direct assignment witness. At the final empty frontier the row yields \(E_C\) and the exact ground multiplicity. Read back the retained original variables and check their actual original clause failures. The ground multiplicity is the model count precisely at grade zero. At positive grade the model count is zero.

## Original Čech terminal return

On a challenged original assignment the nerve in degree \(r\) consists of the ordered \((r+1)\)-tuples of original failed clause indices, with the usual deletion and repetition maps. If the certified grade \(E_C\) is positive then every challenged assignment has a failure. Choose the first original clause failure by scanning the input. In Boolean coefficients the section is \(q_i=e_i\prod_{j<i}(1-e_j)\), with \(e_i(a)=[a\text{ fails }i]\). Prepending its selected index contracts the augmented nerve. Deleting the first entry returns the input simplex, and deleting entry \(j+1\) commutes with prepending after deletion of entry \(j\). This proves the all-degree return constructively. If \(E_C=0\), the saved attaining assignment is the original uncovered point and the original SAT witness.

## Work bound and replay

Let \(N\) denote the original input length and \(w=\max_i|F_i|\) in the supplied clause order. Each transition scans at most \(2^w\) old rows and emits at most \(2^w\) new rows, with at most two candidates for each new row. The moduli have \(O(n)\) bits. Row administration, original label operations, count arithmetic, and finite readback have a polynomial cost in \(N\) per row or input clause. Thus the total bit work, including replay of the ranked trace and one original Čech challenge, is \(2^w\operatorname{poly}(N)\). All loops have a prescribed row or input budget. Charging the remaining rows and administrative steps at their respective polynomial bounds yields a reserve on this execution. This is polynomial for families with \(w=O(\log N)\) in the supplied order.

On the native dyadic tape, the affine inverse branch with word length \(k\) and \(s\) odd steps multiplies differences by \(2^k/3^s\). An output of depth \(m\) therefore needs only \((m-k)_+\) tail digits. A shared tail satisfies all marked uses at the maximum of their demands, and the strong triangle inequality preserves that precision when the returns are added. The row bound above supplies the size of the shared computation for this specific fold.

A uniform deterministic polynomial bound for computing \(E_C\) on every CNF would decide SAT. The converse follows under \(P=NP\) by deciding whether there exists an original assignment of action at most \(k\), then binary searching \(k\). Successive original pins return a minimizing assignment. An exact multiplicity reader would additionally count satisfying assignments. This implementation's unrestricted width can grow linearly with \(N\).

The CLI uses Python 3.10 or later. CNFs are JSON lists of clauses of signed nonzero integer labels. Counts and assignments refer to occurring original variables. Failed-clause indices start at 1. Example inputs and verification:

    printf '%s\n' '[[1],[-1]]' > cnf.json
    printf '%s\n' '{"1":true}' > mark.json
    python3 h24_fold.py cnf.json --trace > derivation.json
    python3 h24_fold.py cnf.json --verify derivation.json --challenge mark.json

The Hets specification gives local proof rules. Supply `replay.commands` to the Hets interactive console to export and replay those goals. The fold, the width argument, the original fibrewise contraction and the mixed coefficient reconstruction are given above with their constructive proofs.
16. The original Čech nerve computes the Hilbert 24 minimum

Complete original source: Original_Cech_Nerve_Hilbert_24.zip :: Original_Cech_Nerve_Hilbert_24/README.md

finite original CNF with supplied clause order; repeated clause text keeps distinct original indices failure opens represented by consistent Boolean pins; contradictory pins are empty original live partial pins and complete polynomial vectors retained through marginalization CRT product greater than 2^n after signed binomial summation H is actual sum of projected live support sizes; no input-polynomial support bound assumed

F_C(u)=sum_I |U_I|u^|I|=sum_a(1+u)^A_C(a); Z_C(t)=F_C(t-1); projected face-table invariant; backward original minimizing assignment; original augmented-nerve contraction; H poly(N) complete bit work

# The original Čech nerve computes the Hilbert 24 minimum

The input is an arbitrary finite, succinct CNF in its supplied original clause order. Occurring variable labels and every original clause position are retained. The fold builds its computation from the intersections of the original failed-clause Čech cover.

## One operator for the original nerve and action

Write \(U_i\) for the original assignments failing original clause \(i\). Represent \(U_i\) by the partial assignment that makes every literal false. Inconsistent pins give the empty open. An empty clause gives the whole ambient assignment space. On a marked native fibre let \(P_i(a)=[a\in U_i]I_4\). These scalar blocks satisfy the original Gram equation \(P_i^\top G=GP_i\) and commute with each of the six native frames, using
\[
G=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&1\\0&2&-1&0\\0&-1&2&0\\1&0&0&2\end{pmatrix}.
\]
The reversible four-bit Rule 30 chart reindexes the marked fibres together with their operators. Every overlap transports by the same reindexing. In dyadic arithmetic the Boolean pins supply this presentation directly.

Set \(U_I=\bigcap_{i\in I}U_i\), with \(U_\varnothing\) the ambient Boolean space. Increasing subsets of original indices represent the alternating Čech faces. Retain their degree \(r=|I|\). Repeated clause text keeps its distinct indices, so equal opens can still contribute an overlap. The ordinary labelled Čech nerve also admits ordered tuples and repetitions, used in its contraction.

The native face sum is
\[
 \prod_i(I_4+uP_i(a))=\sum_{I\subseteq[m]}u^{|I|}\prod_{i\in I}P_i(a).
\]
Read the \(44\) entry on each original mark and sum. With \(A_C(a)=|\{i:a\in U_i\}|\) the resulting polynomial and its translation are
\[
 F_C(u)=\sum_I|U_I|u^{|I|}=\sum_a(1+u)^{A_C(a)},\qquad
 Z_C(t)=F_C(t-1)=\sum_a t^{A_C(a)}.
\]
Thus the constant coefficient after translation is the exact uncovered count, and the first nonzero degree is the Hilbert 24 least action. The corresponding coefficient counts all its original attaining assignments. The same \(Z_C\) is the native Fresnel and ultrametric specialization source. Substituting \(u=-1\) at the native operator level gives the original SAT projector.

## Fold the faces with early projection

After processing the first \(i\) clauses let \(L_i\) be the variables occurring in that prefix and occurring again later. A row is a partial pin \(p\) on \(L_i\) with a coefficient vector in \(u\). Its basis function is the cylinder indicator \([p]\). The table represents
\[
 \sum_{\text{retired variables}}\prod_{j\le i}(1+u\,e_j),\qquad
 e_j(a)=[a\in U_j].
\]
The identity row starts with the empty pin and coefficient \(1\).

At clause \(i\), copy every old row. Also intersect its pin with the original failure pins for \(i\) and multiply that contribution by \(u\). Keep compatible intersections. The two contributions account for faces omitting and including this original index. Every face has exactly one such decomposition, even when two original indices label the same open.

Next retire every variable whose last occurrence is clause \(i\). On a term \(p\) the marginal multiplies its coefficient by \(2^{k}\) where \(k\) is the number of just-retired variables unpinned by \(p\). Delete the retired coordinates and add vectors with equal live pins. Each step therefore preserves the displayed table invariant. The final live set is empty and the one scalar row contains all increasing face masses. Trailing zero face masses may be omitted in the output.

The binomial weights for the action coefficient at degree \(s\) are \((-1)^{r-s}\binom r s\) on face degrees \(r\ge s\). Each of the final coefficients lies in \([0,2^n]\) for \(n\) occurring original variables. Compute the signed binomial sum modulo coprime powers of \(2\) and \(5\), whose product strictly exceeds \(2^n\), and reconstruct the count by CRT. Reconstructing the coefficient after the signed sum keeps all Čech cancellations. The two moduli support the full native Gram dagger because \(\det G=3\). All intermediate face vectors use integer coefficients and the native Gram equation.

## Original witness and Čech contraction

Start at the first nonzero action coefficient. Go backwards through the saved layers. Given the already read values on \(L_i\), look at the layer before it retired its variables. For each newly retired variable count the paths at the current action with that variable pinned to zero. This is the same binomial coefficient read, restricted to compatible pin rows and weighted by the number of remaining assignments. A positive count selects zero. Otherwise the parent count was positive and selects one. Once these variables are read, check clause \(i\) on the chosen original values and subtract its failure bit from the action to carry backwards. The invariant is that the pinned prefix still has an attaining extension at its carried action. At the empty prefix the action is zero. Every original variable was retired once, so the result is a total original minimizing assignment.

If the ground action is zero, this assignment is the original uncovered Čech point. If it is positive then every original assignment is covered. On a challenged original assignment scan for the first failed original clause. Prepending its labelled index to a simplex is an extra degeneracy. The first deletion returns the original simplex, and deleting entry \(j+1\) commutes with prepending after deletion of entry \(j\). This contracts every augmented fibre constructively. The selector is \(q_i=e_i\prod_{j<i}(1-e_j)\) in the native Boolean coefficient algebra.

For \((x)\land(x)\land(\neg x)\), the first two original opens are equal and give a labelled edge. The face polynomial is \(2+3u+u^2\), the action polynomial is \(t+t^2\), and the ground action is \(1\), attained at \(x=1\). A challenge at \(x=0\) returns original clause \(1\).

## Bit work and scope

Let \(N\) be the input length and \(H\) the sum of the projected live support sizes, including the initial identity row. At each clause there are at most two extension terms per old row before grouping. A coefficient vector has at most \(m+1\) entries. The total number of weighted face/assignment incidences at a cut is at most \(2^{m+n}\), so its integers have \(O(m+n)\) bits. Sorting to coalesce equal pins adds only polynomial overhead per row, because its logarithmic factor is \(O(n)\). Replaying the saved terms at most once per retired variable in the backward read costs at most another factor \(n\). The complete work, including CRT, witness return and finite trace verification, is \(H\,\operatorname{poly}(N)\). The fixed implementation can debit a polynomial bound per scheduled row operation. This is a support-sensitive upper bound for the supplied clause order.

For two opposite disjunctions on the same \(n\) variables, the cut width is \(n\) but the nerve fold has two intermediate rows and returns \(2^n-2\) models. In contrast, processing \((u\lor x_i)\) for every \(i\) before \((v\lor x_i)\) for every \(i\) leaves \(2^n\) different pins at the middle cut. The two stars are joined by their shared leaves. This proves the exponential support possibility for this fold and order. A uniform polynomial bound in the original \(N\) for the ground degree on every CNF would decide SAT and yield \(P=NP\).

## Running the fold

Use Python 3.10 or later, with JSON clauses of signed nonzero integer labels. Clause indices start at 1.

    printf '%s\n' '[[1],[1],[-1]]' > cnf.json
    printf '%s\n' '{"1":false}' > mark.json
    python3 cech_fold.py cnf.json --trace > derivation.json
    python3 cech_fold.py cnf.json --verify derivation.json --challenge mark.json

The runtime includes the original nerve coefficients, translated coefficients, CRT residues, original readback, and optional projected trace. The CASL specification and exported E refutations replay the local intersections, weighted and signed face splitting, and contracting-vertex identities. The projection, global induction, complexity statement and modular decoding are the constructive arguments above.
17. Hilbert 24 on a marked reversible Cayley action

Complete original source: Hilbert24_Reversible_Cayley_S4.zip :: h24_cayley/README.md

retain original assignment tags even when evaluation characters coincide; optimize original evaluation subfamily rank construction stated for original 3-CNF failure polynomials finite compiled computation DAG; sufficient live workspace; copying includes complete returned certificate original pinning square cartesian for finite Beck–Chevalley LP witness gluing requires tree of bags with separator marginals explicit original graph/charge data for Tseitin leaf elimination

L_C chi_a=A_C(a)chi_a on original evaluation characters; finite constructive minimum pair; Sigma_p ⊣ p* ⊣ Pi_p and cost max_p ⊣ p* ⊣ min_p; finite Beck–Chevalley; reversible DAG work <=2 sum_v c_v+W_copy; Tseitin minimum equals odd connected-component count; S4 four-sheeted Cayley cover and Golay coordinate action

# Hilbert 24 on a marked reversible Cayley action

For an original CNF \(C\), let \(f_j(a)\in\{0,1\}\) be the indicator that assignment \(a\) fails original clause \(j\). The objective, witness, and certificate are

\[
E_C=\min_a A_C(a),\qquad A_C(a)=\sum_j f_j(a),\qquad
(a_*,\ A_C(a_*)=E_C,\ \forall a\ A_C(a)\ge E_C).
\]

## Literal Cayley realization and original return

Regard the flags as Boolean functions with addition over \(\mathbb F_2\). Let \(W_C\) be their span and let \(R_f\) translate elements of \(W_C\) by \(f\). On the character \(\chi_a(f)=(-1)^{f(a)}\),

\[
L_C\chi_a=A_C(a)\chi_a,\qquad L_C=\tfrac12\sum_j(I-R_{f_j}).
\]

Every summand has the exact original clause mark. The factor \(1/2\) is a positive real edge weighting. The denominator-cleared dyadic identity is \((I-R_{f_j})\chi_a=2f_j(a)\chi_a\). One may keep copies tagged by \(a\), so coincident characters do not identify distinct original assignments. The unrestricted graph always has a constant zero mode. The exact problem is to minimize over the original evaluation characters. For the eight possible signed clauses on a single triple, every assignment is a minimizer of value one and fails its own uniquely labelled clause. None realizes the constant mode.

For 3-CNF, expand each literal failure product in square-free monomials of degree at most three. Row reduction computes a basis of original flags and a generator description in polynomial time. On the failure open of a discarded clause, the XOR expression in basis flags has odd parity. Send the original mark to the first failed basis clause in that expression. Use the identity on retained labels. This defines a retraction of the failed-clause cover on clopen pieces. In the Čech nerve, the original and returned labels over the same assignment lie in the same fibre and have the canonical arrow between them, giving a homotopy from the induced inclusion–retraction to the identity over the assignment space.

An all-character Cayley sparsifier gives the same multiplicative comparison on every marked original action and every pinned branch. It preserves zero exactly for \(\varepsilon<1\). If there are \(m\) clauses, \(\varepsilon<1/(2m+1)\) makes every exact surrogate minimizer an original minimizer. For two consecutive costs \(i,i+1\), the lower edge of the second band exceeds the upper edge of the first. In a worst case, each clause can be the only one failed by some assignment. Then every generator is required for relative error below one.

The basis has rank \(r\), and its original clauses involve at most \(3r\) variables. Every flag depends only on those variables. Thus minimizing on the union of the basis supports and filling unused bits by zero is an exact construction on logarithmic-rank instances. In general \(W_C\) can have \(2^r\) elements. A polynomial original-input-time algorithm for the minimum over original marks, or just for whether it is zero, implies \(P=NP\). Conversely, under \(P=NP\), NP threshold queries compute the exact value by binary search, and pinning one variable at a time constructs a minimizing original mark. A decision 2-CNF leaf can detect zero by SCC. Optimizing a positive failed-clause count on general 2-CNF instead contains Max-Cut, using the pair \((x_u\lor x_v),(\neg x_u\lor\neg x_v)\) for each graph edge.

## Fredkin–Toffoli, adjoints, and the clock

On original assignments and a flag bit the generator is

\[
U_j(a,b)=(a,b\oplus f_j(a)).
\]

For three literal-failure bits \(u,v,w\), a three-Toffoli block computes \(t\mathrel{\oplus}=uv\) into clean scratch, applies \(b\mathrel{\oplus}=tw\), then clears \(t\) by repeating the first gate. Signed literal controls use the corresponding polarity. On the signed flag vector \((|a,0\rangle-|a,1\rangle)\), \(U_j\) has eigenvalue \((-1)^{f_j(a)}\). A Fredkin controlled swap routes the selected original packet after a reversible comparison. A reversible minimum gate retains the operands and writes the value and tie-broken selector into clean registers.

The composition proof is direct: \(U_f U_g(a,b)=(a,f(a)\oplus(g(a)\oplus b))=(a,(f+g)(a)\oplus b)=U_{f+g}(a,b)\). This needs only the associativity of XOR and pointwise evaluation of the sum.

Let \(p:X\to Y\) forget the next marked decision. In finite families, \(\Sigma_p\dashv p^*\dashv\Pi_p\). The left adjoint supplies a branch and its local attaining witness. Pullback reindexes a bound or decision to the original branch. The right adjoint gathers the lower-bound proofs for all branches. For numerical costs with ordinary order,

\[
\max_p\dashv p^*\dashv\min_p,
\qquad b\le\min_p A\iff p^*b\le A.
\]

The min and max switch places in the opposite order. A cartesian restriction by original pins commutes with each fibre construction by the induced bijection on fibres. This is the finite Beck–Chevalley law. In particular, the numerical minimum and the proof pair may be constructed under a base-\(3/2\) pinning and read back in the original assignment coordinates.

Here is the constructive fold. At a complete assignment, return its marked action and the reflexive bound on its singleton fibre. At a binary parent, let the child packages return \((e_0,a_0,\ell_0)\) and \((e_1,a_1,\ell_1)\), where \(\ell_i\) bounds every assignment in child \(i\) below by \(e_i\). Set \(e=\min(e_0,e_1)\), return the marked endpoint of the first child with cost \(e\), and for any challenged assignment use its original next bit \(i\) to compose \(e\le e_i\le A_C(a)\). This proves and attains the original minimum by finite recursion. When suffix subproblems are shared, retain the readback embedding for each use. The construction's cost is the size of the resulting marked computation DAG and the cost of writing its return.

For a fixed finite DAG of compiled computations, keep one register for each shared result. Compute its nodes once in dependency order, copy the required output, then uncompute in reverse order. If \(c_v\) includes a node's local reversible gate cost and its reads, work is bounded by \(2\sum_v c_v+W_{\mathrm{copy}}\). Copy cost includes any returned certificate. This bound uses sufficient live workspace to hold the intermediate results. Reducing that workspace can require recomputation. Multiple uses of an inverse Collatz tail with lengths \(k_i\) and requested dyadic apertures \(m_i\) only require tail depth \(\max_i(m_i-k_i)_+\). That maximum bounds precision and the DAG bounds work.

Time is an execution resource carried by the dependency order. The same endpoint permutation can have runs of arbitrarily different gate counts by insertion of reversible gate–inverse pairs. A positive-cost loop also prevents a strictly decreasing rank on bare configurations. A terminating program must make progress visible in its execution state, for example by its round or remaining schedule. For a fixed compiled DAG, the sum of unexecuted gate costs is an explicit reserve. An all-CNF input-polynomial result would need an input-polynomial bound on constructing and executing a sufficient marked DAG.

## The S4 sector and the 24 Golay coordinates

The three star transpositions \((14),(24),(34)\) give a connected 24-vertex cubic Cayley graph. Under \(S_4\to S_4/V_4\cong S_3\) they map bijectively to the three reflections of \(K_{3,3}\). This is a four-sheeted labelled cover. Its adjacency characteristic polynomial is

\[
x^4(x^2-9)(x^2-4)^6(x^2-1)^3.
\]

The nontrivial spectrum lies between \(-2\) and \(2\), so the cover is Ramanujan. Adding its Laplacian as an independent frame factor leaves every original marked action unchanged on the constant-frame line. In the concrete cubic Tseitin family on this graph, sum the charges in each connected component. One failure is forced in every odd component. Set off-tree edge bits to zero and eliminate leaves in each spanning tree, sending the outstanding parity to its parent. The odd roots give exactly one failed original clause per odd component, proving and attaining the minimum without searching the full assignment cube.

In the hexacode chart of the six Golay tetrads, put \(v=(1,1,\omega,\omega,\omega^2,\omega^2)\). The rotations \(R=(135)(246)\) and \(S=(12)(36)(54)\) with row Frobenius for \(S\) preserve the hexacode. They send \(v\) to \(\omega^2 v\) and \(v\), respectively. The four row translations by \(tv\) are normal under these operations, with the two induced actions on \(t\) given by multiplication by \(\omega^2\) and Frobenius. Thus the group is \(V_4\rtimes S_3\cong S_4\), regular on the 24 coordinates. A row translation adds \(ptv\) to the hexacode label vector for common column parity \(p\). A nonzero translation changes top-row parity by the trace of the Hermitian pairing with that hexacode vector, plus \(6p=0\). The pairing vanishes. Therefore the action preserves the Golay code and, in the same coordinate chart, the Leech congruences. Unique Golay decoding corrects up to three flips in a 24-bit digit block. The original frame and row labels travel with this coordinate chart.

## Point probes, scale, and LP

The original marked probe is the Dirac measure \(\delta_a\). Its normalized Haar detail at each binary depth is supported only on the prefix containing \(a\). A discrete transform linear in sample count can therefore handle a single marked Dirac compactly. The data of all costs can be dense in the same basis. For example a unit clause on the very last bit has \(2^{n-1}\) nonzero finest-scale details.

The six-frame basis \(F_2\otimes F_3\) gives a Butson matrix. Its adjoint product is \(6I\), so exact dyadic inversion costs one extra input digit. The mod-two reduction of \(\mathbb Z_2[\omega]\) supplies the hexacode alphabet. For marked depth coefficients \(h_j\), use \(Z(z)=\sum_j h_j z^{-j}\) and \(M(s)=Z(2^s)\). This is also the Laplace transform of the masses at \(j\log 2\), bilateral for signed depths. At finite aperture, Parseval transfers the same frame-energy bound to the transform, and Fourier coefficient extraction inverts it. A marked inverse Collatz block of length \(k\) shifts depths by \(k\), giving a multiplier \(z^{-k}\) for conditioned Haar. The advanced z-transform adds an independent off-grid sampling offset. The integer dyadic valuation shift requires no such offset.

For a diagonal original action \(Q=\operatorname{diag} A_C\) and positive marked coupling \(L\), the deterministic bound is \(E_C\le\lambda_{\min}(Q+\eta L)\le E_C+\eta\lVert L\rVert\) for \(\eta\ge0\). A Dirac at any minimizing assignment proves the upper bound. Below half a unit the integer value can be recovered by rounding an exact energy read. The nearly diagonal random-matrix paper studies a different, ensemble perturbation problem.

A finite exact explicit LP minimizes expected original clause action over distributions on compatible paths. On a tree of bags, match separator marginals. A root row of positive mass can be extended to a compatible positive-mass row in every child, so the distributions glue. The optimum returns an original minimizing assignment. With width \(w\) there are \(O(|T|2^{w+1})\) bag rows. On the four cubic parity bags of \(K_4\) with odd total charge, uniform distributions on individual valid triples agree on shared edge marginals and have objective zero, while the original minimum is one. Strongly polynomial LP therefore applies once an exact explicit LP has been constructed.

Run `python minimum.py Eight_Clauses.json` for a worked example. The solver returns minimum 1, assignment 000, failed original clause 1, and seven separator-row comparisons. Each clause is a factor table. For each eliminated bit the program sums the factors containing that bit, minimizes locally, and saves the chosen bit for each separator row. Factors outside the bucket pass through unchanged. Induction proves the remaining tables give the exact minimum conditioned on the remaining bits. Reverse substitution attains it. The printed comparison count covers the explicitly processed separator rows. Large separators can make these tables exponential. This implementation evaluates the exact tables directly. The reversible circuit construction above describes how to compile those computations while keeping their readback.

Run `python checks.py` for the finite action identities, the flag circuit, the Golay action on all 4,096 words, the exact frame spectrum, the original Tseitin examples, and exhaustive comparisons of the minimum constructor on 300 generated formulas.

`Marked_Cayley_H24.het` formalizes the local flag transport, exact cleared clause energy, and marked minimum step. Hets with SPASS closed eight local goals. E proved composition separately from the three equations above. The replay scope is local. The adjoint, execution cost, and graph-lift arguments are given in this note.

18. Belnap, Čech, Cayley and the exact original Hilbert 24 return — full source

%% Belnap FOUR x FOUR -> marked source -> Cech -> exact Cayley sparsification.
%% Question = retained queried assertion / cut. Spectrum = marked bit or
%% Walsh character, not an unlabelled multiset. Readout goes to the same
%% original source point, not to an arbitrary representative.
%%
%% Written proof of the infinite statement (not an axiom of the CASL core):
%% X = {0,1}^N x Product_{r>=1} K(Z/2,r), with Cantor-cylinder site on
%% the first factor. Fix original finite CNF C; U_j is the clopen on which
%% original clause j fails, pulled back to X. For each x the fiber of the
%% realization of the Cech nerve of coproduct U_j -> X is the realization
%% of the indiscrete groupoid on J_x = {j : x in U_j}. If J_x is empty
%% the fiber is empty. If nonempty, choose its least original index j;
%% inserting it as the first vertex contracts the augmented nerve.
%% The choice is locally constant at the finite clause aperture. Thus
%% this gives actual coherent contractions over X, in the slice, at all
%% simplicial degrees. Conversely an equivalence has nonempty fibers;
%% any satisfying assignment extends by zero bits and the basepoints of
%% all K's and gives an empty fiber. Hence equivalence iff UNSAT. This
%% works on the fixed infinite base, not only on a sampled finite table.
%%
%% A total signed constructor exists: recursively test the two cofactors,
%% glue two negative sections or return the satisfying child and its bit.
%% This proves the construction without a complexity hypothesis; the
%% straightforward tree may use 2^n leaves. A uniform polynomial-time
%% signed return (positive original SAT model or negative original Cech
%% equivalence) decides SAT, hence P=NP by Cook-Levin. A sound negative
%% producer with one polynomial deadline on every UNSAT instance already
%% suffices by timeout. These deductions are not P != NP claims.
%%
%% Tseitin at arbitrary aperture, exact generator lemma:
%% Fix pinned edges J with values y. Let H=(V,E\J), and b_y be the original
%% charge plus the incidence boundary of y. The remaining edge moves add
%% g_uv=e_u+e_v to the syndrome in F_2^V. For a cut mark S the Walsh
%% character is chi_S(r)=(-1)^{sum_{v in S}r_v}. Its eigenvalue for the
%% average generator walk (if H has edges) is 1-2|delta_H(S)|/|E(H)|.
%% The fixed characters are exactly unions of H-components; with no
%% edges every character is fixed. Their phase on the actual source is
%% (-1)^b_y(S). A fixed character of phase -1 gives a certificate using
%% precisely the marked original vertex equations, with pin values included.
%% Indeed each unread edge occurs twice or zero times in that sum. The
%% original challenged assignment necessarily violates one of those
%% vertex equations; its actual incident tuple names an original false
%% clause in the standard forbidden-tuple CNF.
%%
%% Exact sparsifier and return (polynomial in original graph size):
%% Use a spanning forest F of H. Each omitted edge generator g_uv equals
%% the F_2 sum of the generators on the forest path from u to v, by
%% cancelling every internal vertex twice. Thus the generator spans and
%% all fixed marked modes agree. Keep the original edge list and the F
%% section; any source solution can be constructed on the forest by leaf
%% elimination (set nonforest unread bits zero). The leaf edge must have
%% the leaf charge; adding it at the neighbor leaves the component total
%% unchanged. Hence the last charge vanishes iff the component total
%% was even. An odd component gives an original refutation. Optional
%% count: 0 in that case, otherwise 2^(|E(H)|-|V|+number of components);
%% each nonforest assignment lifts uniquely by the same elimination.
%% No Cayley vertex list is constructed. A forest taken before arbitrary
%% pins need not retain the component partition after the pins.
%%
%% Exact Cayley construction on EVERY finite CNF (not only Tseitin):
%% Index points by G=F_2^n at the original finite aperture. After
%% removing tautologies and repeated literals per clause, clause j
%% fails on a single marked local pattern b on coordinates I.
%% Let X_i denote shift by e_i on functions on G. Then
%%       Q_j = product_{i in I} (Id + (-1)^b_i X_i)/2.
%% On the character marked by assignment a, its eigenvalue is the
%% product of bits [a_i=b_i]. Thus each Q_j is an orthogonal projector;
%% an empty clause gives Id and a tautology gives 0. Writing W for
%% Walsh transform and D_C for the diagonal count of original failures,
%%       T_C = sum_j Q_j = W D_C W^{-1}.
%% The zero characters and only those return original satisfying
%% assignments. A nonzero eigenvalue has a positive summand naming
%% an original failure. Thus nullity equals the number of solutions;
%% full rank is the exact Cech statement. Each width-k term uses
%% at most 2^k shifts when expanded, or k retained factors.
%% A frame graph can be inserted without changing the target: for any
%% connected Cayley graph on K (for instance the six S3 frames, with
%% generators r,r^{-1},s), take T_C tensor Id + Id tensor L_K.
%% Both summands are PSD. A zero vector is in both kernels, so the
%% new kernel is exactly ker(T_C) tensor constants. A relative spectral
%% sparsifier of the frame graph preserves that exact marked return.
%% Conversely a standard unsigned Laplacian on G cannot generally
%% realize T_C: for (x1 OR x2) the kernel dimension is 3, but an
%% unsigned Cayley Laplacian on F_2^2 has nullity [G:subgroup] in {1,2,4}.
%% Positivity of a matrix operator is not positivity of generator weights.
%%
%% Weighted cuts, Ramanujan and exact return:
%% For two consistent original charges, T is the vertex set where they
%% differ. A cut question distinguishes them exactly iff |S intersect T|
%% is odd. If H is any cut sparsifier with a*wG(delta S)<=wH(delta S)
%% <=b*wG(delta S) and a>0, minimizing over the SAME odd vertex sets in H
%% returns an original distinguishing mark of cost <=(b/a)*opt_G:
%% a*wG(S_H)<=wH(S_H)<=wH(S_G)<=b*wG(S_G). A zero minimum is preserved.
%% Read parity on the original cut, not on the retained edge list.
%% Padberg-Rao gives polynomial weighted odd-cut optimization on the
%% explicit original graph; the sparsifier can shorten a graph, not remove
%% the marked parity constraint. A d-regular Ramanujan on V gives
%% |delta S| >= (d-lambda_2)*|S|*(1-|S|/|V|), by the Rayleigh quotient of
%% 1_S - (|S|/|V|)*1. This regulates CUT COST on the vertex graph. It
%% does not assert the syndrome Cayley graph itself is Ramanujan.
%%
%% Exact limitation: use the same assignment Cayley graph for CNFs
%% (x_1) AND (not x_1) and (x_1) AND (x_1). Its unmarked Laplacian is
%% identical, but the Cech cover is onto only for the first. Even the
%% marked operator L+D with D the diagonal of failure counts is positive
%% definite on a connected graph whenever D is nonzero: zero energy
%% forces a constant via L, then forces that constant to vanish via D.
%% The second CNF therefore still has positive-definite L+D.
%% The exact marked operator instead is D alone: ker D has one basis
%% vector per model. A relative Loewner sparsifier of D preserves ker D;
%% for weighted retained clause projectors, every point covered by the
%% full family remains covered, so find an original failure in the
%% retained list. This only promises an exact reduction of the cover
%% when supplied that domination; it does not decide the new kernel
%% succinctly. If decomposed into pointwise rank-one terms, a full cover
%% has rank 2^n, so the usual rank-one sparsification must retain at
%% least 2^n terms. Keep grouped clause operators or exact Cayley moves.
%%
%% References:
%% https://arxiv.org/abs/0808.0163 (weighted Laplacian sparsification)
%% https://web.math.princeton.edu/~nalon/PDFS/exp1.pdf (Cayley expansion)
%% https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/moor.7.1.67 (odd cut)
%% https://www.claymath.org/library/monographs/MPPc.pdf (complexity target)

spec BCBit =
  free type Bit ::= b0 | b1
  op bxor : Bit * Bit -> Bit
  . bxor(b0,b0) = b0
  . bxor(b0,b1) = b1
  . bxor(b1,b0) = b1
  . bxor(b1,b1) = b0
end

spec BCFour =
  BCBit then
  free type Obs ::= obs(Bit; Bit)
  ops support, oppose : Obs -> Bit;
      negate : Obs -> Obs
  forall p,n : Bit
  . support(obs(p,n)) = p
  . oppose(obs(p,n)) = n
  . negate(obs(p,n)) = obs(n,p)
end

spec BCFrame =
  BCFour then
  free type Frame ::= cell(Obs; Obs)
  ops question, spectral : Frame -> Obs;
      swapFrame : Frame -> Frame
  forall q,s : Obs
  . question(cell(q,s)) = q
  . spectral(cell(q,s)) = s
  . swapFrame(cell(q,s)) = cell(s,q)
end

spec BCFrameReturns =
  BCFrame then
  forall q : Obs; f : Frame
  . negate(negate(q)) = q %(belnap_negation_involution)% %implied
  . swapFrame(swapFrame(f)) = f %(cayley_swap_involution)% %implied
  . cell(question(f),spectral(f)) = f %(all_sixteen_cells_roundtrip)% %implied
end

spec BCSourceFiber =
  BCFrame then
  sorts Point, Clause, Mark
  ops anchor : Mark -> Point;
      original : Mark -> Clause;
      observe : Mark -> Frame
  preds fails : Point * Clause;
        model, inhabited : Point
  forall x : Point; m : Mark
  . model(x) <=> not (exists k : Clause . fails(x,k))
  . inhabited(x) <=> (exists k : Clause . fails(x,k))
  . (support(spectral(observe(m))) = b1) <=> fails(anchor(m),original(m))
end

spec BCSourceReturns =
  BCSourceFiber then
  forall x : Point; m : Mark
  . inhabited(x) <=> not model(x) %(fiber_iff_not_model)% %implied
  . (support(spectral(observe(m))) = b1) => fails(anchor(m),original(m)) %(marked_original_clause_return)% %implied
  . (forall y : Point . inhabited(y)) <=> not (exists y : Point . model(y)) %(global_cover_iff_unsat)% %implied
end

spec BCGeneratorPairs =
  BCBit then
  sorts Vertex, Cut
  ops member : Cut * Vertex -> Bit;
      seam : Cut * Vertex * Vertex -> Bit
  forall S : Cut; u,v : Vertex
  . seam(S,u,v) = bxor(member(S,u),member(S,v))
end

spec BCGeneratorReturns =
  BCGeneratorPairs then
  forall S : Cut; u,v,w : Vertex
  . seam(S,u,v) = seam(S,v,u) %(same_undirected_cut)% %implied
  . seam(S,u,u) = b0 %(loop_character_trivial)% %implied
  . bxor(seam(S,u,v),seam(S,v,w)) = seam(S,u,w) %(cayley_path_telescope)% %implied
  . (seam(S,u,v) = b0 /\ seam(S,v,w) = b0) => seam(S,u,w) = b0 %(zero_mode_preserved_under_shortcut)% %implied
end

%% Native Rule 30 step and paracollatznormalized guard
%% Programme instruction: working in the tesseract means carrying the
%% Rule 30 dynamics and their original-state readback, not just using
%% a static 16-state table. One exact Boolean chart is
%% E(l,c,r,b) = cell(obs(l,c),obs(r,b)); b is a retained target bit.
%% This packs four coordinates and does not identify an opposition
%% rail with the complement of a support rail.
%% On b=0 the fourth readout is f(l,c,r)=l xor (c or r), i.e. Rule 30.
%% For arbitrary b the full four-coordinate gate is an involution.
%% On two row tracks the corresponding involution is (x,y) ->
%% (x,y xor F30(x)), with F30 computed from the unchanged source x.
%% Reading its second track at y=0 yields the original next row.
%% At multiple ticks the causal apertures must match: a radius-R
%% output at time t is determined by the radius-(R+t) source window.
%% This follows by induction since each output uses radius one.
%% Thus compatible aperture calculations give the same infinite orbit,
%% in particular the same center for the original single seed.
%% This encodes Rule 30 and its readback, not a periodicity result.
%%
%% At each digit, encode all four rails with the seven nonzero
%% F_2^3 columns as parity-check labels. Use the identity columns
%% for three check bits; the other four columns label the payload.
%% A one-bit error in position j has syndrome column j, identifying
%% exactly the repair; zero syndrome on the allowed noise model
%% means no error. This repairs at most one bit per seven-bit block
%% and commutes with restriction to whole digit blocks. Decode,
%% move by the very same Rule 30 gate, and re-encode: on a corrected
%% packet this transports the original update without losing the mark.
%% A kernel version with isometric encoding K is
%% Tguard = K TC K* + (Id - K K*); on the admitted image it is TC,
%% outside it is the identity. Hence ker(Tguard)=K ker(TC).
%% This protects the original decision readout; it doesn't decide it.

spec BCRule30Data =
  BCFrame then
  op bor : Bit * Bit -> Bit
  ops localRule : Bit * Bit * Bit -> Bit;
      rule30Gate : Frame -> Frame
  . bor(b0,b0) = b0
  . bor(b0,b1) = b1
  . bor(b1,b0) = b1
  . bor(b1,b1) = b1
  forall l,c,r,b : Bit
  . localRule(l,c,r) = bxor(l,bor(c,r))
  . rule30Gate(cell(obs(l,c),obs(r,b))) =
      cell(obs(l,c),obs(r,bxor(b,localRule(l,c,r))))
end

spec BCRule30Returns =
  BCRule30Data then
  forall l,c,r,b : Bit; f : Frame
  . rule30Gate(rule30Gate(f)) = f %(rule30_reversible_lift)% %implied
  . oppose(spectral(rule30Gate(cell(obs(l,c),obs(r,b0))))) =
      localRule(l,c,r) %(rule30_original_boolean_readback)% %implied
  . question(rule30Gate(f)) = question(f) %(rule30_question_retained)% %implied
  . support(spectral(rule30Gate(f))) = support(spectral(f))
      %(rule30_right_input_retained)% %implied
end

%% Hilbert 24 applied directly to the FULL P versus NP test
%% -------------------------------------------------------
%% Input is the original, succinct finite CNF C, including clause
%% indices. No promise on class, treewidth, satisfiability or symmetry.
%% For n input variables each path sets the bits of an original
%% assignment a. Assign nonnegative unit action to each failed original
%% clause and let A_C(a) be the total. The proof-valued path polynomial
%% is Z_C(t) = sum_a t^A_C(a) with a retained as coefficient witness.
%% E_C=min_a A_C(a) is the ground action. The constant coefficient
%% inhabits SAT; it is empty iff the original Cech map is an equivalence.
%% Keep the proof that the returned action bounds every assignment, not
%% merely an assignment of that cost (which gives the opposite bound).
%%
%% Exact fold without expanding the path polynomial:
%% Start with the original clause functions, each returning 0 or 1.
%% At variable x, take precisely the current factors that mention x.
%% Replace them by h(s)=min_{b in {0,1}} sum_{f in bucket} f(b,s),
%% and keep a minimizing b for each boundary assignment s. Unrelated
%% factors are unchanged since they do not depend on b. This is exactly
%% min-plus distributivity with independent factors; if a variable is
%% absent choose b=0. After all variables, sum the remaining constants.
%% For every original assignment a, each replacement weakly decreases
%% its total: the original value b is a competitor. Chain these local
%% inequalities to obtain the global certificate E_C <= A_C(a).
%% For an attained optimum reverse the elimination order, taking the
%% stored minimizing bit at the already reconstructed boundary each
%% time. Every inequality becomes equality, so the original witness a
%% has A_C(a)=E_C. This is the calibrated Hilbert 24 pair: original
%% lower proof plus attaining path, not an estimate or a bare grade.
%% At E_C=0 the path is a model. At E_C>0 and any challenged original a
%% the lower proof forces at least one failed original clause; scan its
%% original clauses for the first failure, constant on the corresponding
%% finite aperture. Inserting it contracts the original Cech fiber.
%% The 4x4 Rule 30 chart and checked guard can transport these marked
